EVENT: Q2 2026 earnings
STATE_TRANSITION: Improvement
IMPORTANCE: **
MARKET_REACTION: Positive
MARKET_REACTION_DETAIL: Shares rose as much as approximately 2.4% as investors focused on improving European steel economics and the benefits of the new trade framework.
PM_READ: ArcelorMittal provides the first direct evidence that Europe's tighter steel-import regime and CBAM are improving domestic utilization and margin expectations, although U.S. tariffs remain a material offset elsewhere in the portfolio.
THEME_BUCKETS: Industrial Cycle, Europe Demand
THEMES: European steel, TRQ, CBAM, import quotas, capacity utilization, shipments, tariffs
SEGMENT_TABLE_STATUS: Partial
SOURCE_ROLE: Brief note

EXEC SUMMARY

Q2 sales were **$16.76B**, not $16.1B.

EBITDA reached $2.06B, equivalent to $155/t, versus $1.68B and $131/t in Q1.

Net income was $683M and EPS $0.90.

Net debt ended the quarter at $9.5B.

EU / TARIFF READ

The key datapoint is Europe.

European EBITDA per tonne improved by **$28 sequentially**.

Management says the new tariff-rate-quota system implemented July 1, together with CBAM, is producing stronger order books and allowing capacity restarts.

European Q3 shipments are now expected to be **stable to slightly higher sequentially**, versus the normal high-single-digit seasonal decline.

That is stronger evidence than simply quoting an absolute Europe EBITDA/t level.

The supplied $98/t figure is not necessary for the thesis and is omitted because the official release emphasizes the $28/t sequential improvement.

The read-through is not universally tariff-positive.

ArcelorMittal separately estimates U.S. steel tariffs continue to cost approximately $150M per quarter, principally through Canadian exports.

STATE TRANSITION

**CYCLICAL TROUGH RECOVERY / TRADE-TOOL SUPPORTED**

Europe is beginning to behave differently from the normal seasonal steel cycle as import protection and CBAM improve domestic utilization.

Falsification:

- European EBITDA/t reverses the Q2 sequential gain;
- Q3 shipments decline materially despite management's stable-to-higher guide;
- import pricing pressure overwhelms the quota framework.

BOTTOM LINE

ArcelorMittal is a useful policy read-through rather than a core earnings tape.

The important message is that the EU's new steel trade architecture is already changing order books, capacity utilization and expected shipment seasonality.

That makes the European steel trough look increasingly **policy-supported rather than purely cyclical**.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

H1 revenue was €38.64B.

Reported revenue declined 3%.

Organic revenue increased 2%.

LVMH separately disclosed approximately -5% currency impact and -1% perimeter impact. These are rounded official components and should not be mechanically forced to add exactly to the reported percentage.

Q2 organic revenue growth accelerated to 3%, versus approximately 1% in Q1.

Excluding the Middle East conflict, management estimates Q2 organic growth at approximately 4%.

Consensus:

The key consensus miss was Fashion & Leather Goods.

Q2 organic growth was +1%, the first positive quarter in roughly two years, but market expectations were around +1.7%.

The significance is therefore not the magnitude of the beat/miss but the state transition from persistent contraction into positive growth.

EPS / Earnings:

Group net income attributable to shareholders was €5.70B, essentially flat year over year.

Recurring operating profit declined 4% to €8.69B.

Management attributes a substantial portion of the profit decline to currencies.

Profitability / Margins:

Group recurring operating margin remained 22.5%.

Fashion & Leather Goods recurring operating profit declined 7% to €6.20B.

Its recurring operating margin was **34.1%**.

That remains exceptionally high and demonstrates that the core luxury economics have not structurally broken despite the multi-quarter demand slowdown.

Watches & Jewelry recurring operating profit increased 9% to €831M.

Wines & Spirits profit increased 11% to €582M.

Perfumes & Cosmetics profit declined 2% to €417M.

Key KPI:

The defining KPI is **Fashion & Leather Goods Q2 organic growth at +1%**.

This ended seven consecutive quarters of decline.

Management disclosed that Louis Vuitton and Dior were both positive in Q2.

Dior ran somewhat above the divisional average, supported by the early response to Jonathan Anderson's collections.

Loro Piana and Rimowa continued to outperform.

Guidance headline:

Management expects Fashion & Leather Goods to grow at constant currency over full-year 2026.

But CFO Cecile Cabanis explicitly warned that H2 comparisons will become optically tougher.

The correct forward framing is therefore:

**early recovery acknowledged, acceleration not guaranteed.**

Capital allocation:

Operating free cash flow was €4.10B, up 2%.

Net financial debt fell 19% to €8.25B.

LVMH announced an interim dividend of €5.50 per share.

The group also continued portfolio pruning, including the agreed Marc Jacobs disposal and reductions in DFS exposure.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Clean, with significant FX translation distortion.

Adjustment issue:

The main analytical distortion is not a one-off accounting gain.

It is the difference between:

**reported growth -3%**
versus
**organic growth +2%.**

FX reduced reported revenue growth by approximately 5 percentage points.

Management also expects roughly €1B of negative FX impact on FY26 operating profit.

PM interpretation:

For European luxury, reported revenue is currently a poor standalone demand indicator.

The proper hierarchy is:

**organic growth → geographic/client mix → divisional margin → reported growth → FX translation.**

The operating state improved more than the reported -3% headline suggests.

Unlike the U.S. season's equity marks or tariff refunds, however, FX is a recurring economic translation issue rather than an accounting-quality problem.

SEGMENT / BUSINESS BREAKDOWN

SEGMENT TABLE

|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|

| Wines & Spirits | 6.7% | €2.60B | Flat reported; +5% organic | Profit +11% | Early recovery in Cognac and Champagne, including improving China momentum. |

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The most important development is that LVMH's recovery is becoming **product-led rather than purely macro-led**.

Dior's first Jonathan Anderson collections are already generating measurable acceleration.

Louis Vuitton remains supported by its Monogram franchise and new flagship activity.

Tiffany and Bvlgari continue to demonstrate that iconic, ultra-premium products are outperforming the broader aspirational-luxury market.

That matters because luxury recoveries driven only by Chinese macro stimulus or tourism flows are less durable than recoveries built on renewed product desirability.

At the same time, management is correctly avoiding aggressive extrapolation.

H2 comparisons get harder.

Europe remains soft.

Currencies remain a roughly €1B FY operating-profit headwind.

Revision direction: **Mix-shift revisions / modest upward organic-sales revisions, offset by FX.**

Fashion & Leather Goods organic assumptions should move modestly higher.

Watches & Jewelry estimates should move higher.

Reported revenue and EBIT estimates remain constrained by FX.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

LVMH opens the European build with a fundamentally different accounting lens from the U.S. season.

For U.S. companies, the recurring issue has been unusual gains, tariff refunds and GAAP/non-GAAP adjustments.

For European multinationals, the first filter has to be:

**reported growth versus organic growth versus FX.**

LVMH demonstrates why.

A headline -3% sales decline sits on top of +2% organic growth and a sequential acceleration to +3% in Q2.

The second important signal is geographic.

The luxury bear case has remained centered on China.

But H1 2026 shows:

**Asia ex-Japan +6%.**
**Japan +5%.**
**U.S. +4%, accelerating to +6% in Q2.**
**Europe -1%.**

The weakest major region is now Europe, not Asia.

That is a genuine inversion of the 2024-25 luxury debate.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The highest-quality positive signal is the return of Fashion & Leather Goods to positive organic growth.

The second is the breadth of the geographic improvement.

The third is Watches & Jewelry at +11% Q2 organic growth.

The strongest caution is magnitude.

+1% F&LG is an inflection but not yet a full recovery.

The market therefore has a rational reason to wait.

The next print needs to establish persistence rather than simply show stabilization.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

The prior luxury debate was dominated by Chinese consumer weakness, aspirational normalization and doubts about creative renewal at several major brands.

H1 materially weakens that bear case.

Asia ex-Japan is again the strongest geography.

U.S. demand accelerated.

Dior's new creative cycle is producing tangible sales improvement.

But Fashion & Leather Goods is only +1% in Q2, leaving the scale of the recovery unresolved.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

The stock still fell approximately 1.5%.

That is not inconsistent with improving fundamentals.

Fashion & Leather Goods grew only 1% against a market expectation around 1.7%.

The market therefore received evidence of a bottom but not yet evidence of a sufficiently strong recovery to reverse a major de-rating.

This is a classic **unpaid early inflection**.

The underlying setup is nevertheless becoming asymmetric.

The stock had lost roughly 30% year to date.

The core division has turned positive.

Asia and the U.S. are improving.

Margins remain above 34% in Fashion & Leather Goods.

Cash flow remains strong.

The re-rating trigger is now relatively clear:

**F&LG organic growth of roughly 3%-4% or better in Q3, with Watches & Jewelry remaining double-digit and margins broadly stable.**

That would upgrade the evidence from stabilization to recovery.

The thesis weakens materially if F&LG returns negative or Asia ex-Japan rolls over.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder; premium consumer; turnaround / self-help; setup-risk stock.**

The franchise quality has not changed.

What is changing is the cyclical direction of demand after a prolonged luxury normalization.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

Paris-listed LVMH shares fell approximately 1.5% on July 28.

The reaction was fundamentally mild rather than a rejection of the recovery thesis.

The market's issue was that +1% F&LG organic growth remained below the recovery rate investors wanted to see.

The read-across for European luxury is nevertheless constructive.

The strongest part of the market remains the highest-end customer.

Watches & Jewelry grew 11% in Q2.

This matches the broader global premium-consumer evidence from:

premium airline cabins;
high-end travel;
cross-border card spending;
premium consumer electronics.

The aspirational tier remains considerably weaker.

That is the luxury expression of the same K-shaped consumer evident in the U.S.

GEOGRAPHIC / PREMIUM-CONSUMER CHECK

H1 geography:

Asia excluding Japan: approximately +6%.

Japan: approximately +5%.

United States: approximately +4%, with Q2 around +6%.

Europe: approximately -1%.

This creates two important cross-market read-throughs.

First:

**AAPL China + LVMH Asia ex-Japan strength**

both point toward improvement in the premium Chinese consumer.

Second:

**LVMH Europe softness versus U.S. acceleration**

suggests the current luxury problem is no longer well described by a simple "China slowdown" framework.

The market is increasingly dealing with a regional and customer-tier mix issue.

MIDDLE EAST / FX CHECK

The Middle East conflict reduced Q2 organic growth by approximately 1 percentage point.

This is a direct demand-side transmission from geopolitical disruption into luxury tourism and Gulf retail.

Currencies are the larger financial distortion.

FX reduced H1 reported revenue growth by approximately 5 percentage points and is expected to reduce FY26 operating profit by roughly €1B.

For subsequent European earnings notes, the organic/reported/FX bridge should therefore be treated as mandatory.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Fashion & Leather Goods organic growth / Why it matters: A second positive quarter above roughly 3% would convert stabilization into a credible recovery.

2. Dior / Why it matters: Jonathan Anderson's early success must persist beyond initial launch effects to validate the creative-renewal thesis.

3. Asia ex-Japan / Why it matters: Continued growth would confirm that the premium Chinese/Asian consumer has genuinely bottomed.

4. Europe / Why it matters: Continued contraction would make the home region the principal geographic drag.

5. FX / Why it matters: Approximately €1B of FY operating-profit headwind can obscure a genuine organic recovery in reported numbers.

BOTTOM LINE

LVMH delivered an **early but genuine improvement**.

Group organic growth accelerated from 1% to 3%.

Fashion & Leather Goods turned positive for the first time in seven quarters.

The U.S. and Asia improved.

Jewelry remained exceptionally strong.

Margins and cash generation stayed resilient.

The market nevertheless refused to pay for the inflection because **+1% F&LG growth is evidence of stabilization, not yet evidence of a full luxury recovery**.

That distinction is important.

The bear case has shifted.

It is no longer primarily:

**"China demand is collapsing."**

It is now:

**"Is the recovery strong enough and durable enough to offset FX and tougher H2 comparisons?"**

The next re-rating threshold is relatively clean:

**F&LG at approximately +3%-4% organic or better for a second consecutive positive quarter, with jewelry still strong and margins holding.**

Until then, the state is **Improvement — early-stage and still unpaid**.

<<MC.PA_EARNINGS_END>>

new handbags gaining traction;

It delivered that threshold.

Stock identity:

Deep value / turnaround / setup-risk stock.

Kering delivered the European luxury season's **largest positive state transition**.

With debt falling sharply and margins stabilizing, the turnaround has moved from **hope to early evidence**.

Novo reports Q2 obesity-care sales of DKK 23.15B, with US obesity care +4% CER and International Operations +37%; Wegovy pill contributed DKK 3.218B globally.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 reported sales were DKK 78.49B, up 2% in Danish kroner and 3% at CER.

Adjusted sales were also DKK 78.49B in absolute current-quarter terms because there was no Q2 2026 340B adjustment.

The adjusted growth rate is nevertheless **7% at CER**, because Novo removes the DKK 2.65B Q2 2025 340B reversal from the comparison base.

This distinction is essential.

The Q2 operating trajectory is therefore materially better than the reported +3% CER headline suggests.

Consensus:

Adjusted operating profit of DKK 33.39B materially exceeded the DKK 28.74B company-compiled mean forecast.

Wegovy pill sales of approximately DKK 3.22B were slightly below the approximately DKK 3.3B analyst expectation.

Reliable group-sales consensus was not confirmed and is therefore not inserted.

EPS / Earnings:

Reported Q2 net profit was DKK 20.99B, down 21%.

Reported diluted EPS was DKK 4.75, down 20%.

Adjusted net profit was DKK 27.42B, up 5%.

Adjusted diluted EPS was DKK 6.18, up 5%.

Profitability / Margins:

Reported operating profit was DKK 27.06B, down 16% at CER.

Adjusted operating profit was DKK 33.39B, up 11% at CER.

Reported operating margin was 34.5%.

Adjusted operating margin was 42.5%.

Adjusted gross margin was 78.2%.

The operating improvement came from higher sales and lower quarterly costs, while pricing remains the principal structural headwind.

Key KPI:

The central KPI is now the **price-volume conversion of the total Wegovy franchise**.

The pill is a clear volume success.

Weekly pill prescriptions exceeded 265,000 by mid-July, and cumulative prescriptions exceeded 5M.

But quarterly pill sales were only approximately DKK 3.22B and slightly missed elevated market expectations.

Meanwhile, US injectable Wegovy sales declined 22% at CER because lower realized prices more than offset higher volumes.

That split captures the investment debate:

**excellent patient uptake versus weakening revenue per patient.**

Guidance headline:

Adjusted FY26 sales growth is now expected at **0% to -6% CER**.

Adjusted FY26 operating-profit growth is also expected at **0% to -6% CER**.

The previous ranges were -4% to -12% for both metrics.

Novo says the improvement is driven by higher expected GLP-1 sales.

On a non-adjusted basis, the midpoint corresponds to approximately +5% sales growth and +12% operating-profit growth at CER because the large Q1 340B provision reversal remains in reported figures.

Capital allocation:

H1 shareholder distributions totaled DKK 41.2B:

DKK 35.3B in dividends.

DKK 5.9B in share repurchases.

This directly contradicts the preliminary tape's phrase **"capital-return doctrine break."**

There is no evidence of a capital-return break in Q2.

The business continues returning substantial cash while funding significant R&D and manufacturing investment.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Distorted but transparently reconciled.

Adjustment issue:

Two items dominate comparability.

**1. Pipeline impairments**

Q2 contained DKK 6.3B of non-recurring, non-cash intangible-asset impairment charges.

Monlunabant accounted for DKK 4.0B.

Total IFRS impairment losses and reversals on intangible assets were DKK 6.52B.

**2. 340B provision reversals**

Q2 2025 contained a DKK 2.65B 340B rebate-provision reversal.

Q1 2026 contained a much larger DKK 26.76B reversal.

Q2 2026 itself contained no 340B reversal.

The adjustments therefore distort both quarter-on-quarter and year-on-year reported growth, especially at H1 level.

PM interpretation:

Adjusted sales, adjusted operating profit and adjusted EPS are the correct operating measures.

This is not a low-quality earnings beat.

After stripping the impairment and 340B effects, adjusted operating profit still rose 11% at CER.

The market's negative response was therefore not an accounting-quality judgment.

It was a judgment on **future franchise quality**: pill monetization, pricing, competition and pipeline succession.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The guidance raise is significant because it follows an adjusted Q1 in which sales declined 4% at CER.

Q2 adjusted sales then grew 7% and adjusted obesity care grew 16%.

The company is therefore demonstrating a genuine sequential commercial improvement.

But the quality of that improvement is mixed.

Volume is doing more work.

Price is doing less.

The US MFN/access framework expands addressable patient access while explicitly lowering economics per prescription.

That can still be value-accretive if volumes respond sufficiently.

The investment case therefore increasingly resembles a classic price-elasticity problem:

**lower price → more access → more patients → can volume growth exceed price erosion?**

Revision direction: **Upward near-term operating revisions / mix-shift longer-term revisions.**

FY26 sales and operating-profit estimates move upward following the guidance raise.

US realized-pricing assumptions remain under pressure.

Longer-term forecasts remain dependent on pill persistence and pipeline succession.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

The most important feature of Q2 is the divergence between the **operating print** and the **equity reaction**.

Operating data improved substantially.

Adjusted sales accelerated from -4% CER in Q1 to +7% in Q2.

Adjusted operating profit grew 11%.

Obesity care grew 16%.

Guidance was raised sharply.

Yet the ADR fell 6%.

The reason is that Novo is no longer being valued on whether current Wegovy demand exists.

That question is settled.

The market is asking whether Novo can defend the **economics and duration** of the franchise against three pressures:

**pricing; Lilly; next-generation pipeline quality.**

The Wegovy pill illustrates the new bar perfectly.

DKK 3.22B of first-half ramp revenue would ordinarily look impressive for a newly launched product.

At Novo's current setup, being slightly below DKK 3.3B expectations was enough to outweigh a major group operating-profit beat.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The highest-quality positive signal is **Q2 adjusted sales +7% CER after Q1 -4%**.

That is a genuine state change.

The second is obesity care +16%, with International Operations +37%.

The third is the FY guidance raise.

The most important negative signal is **price realization**.

US obesity-care sales grew only 4% despite strong volume.

US injectable Wegovy revenue declined 22% at CER.

The second negative signal is pipeline credibility.

The monlunabant impairment, failed ZEUS cardiovascular trial and CagriSema comparison keep pressure on Novo to prove that growth can persist beyond the current semaglutide franchise.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q1 showed strong Wegovy pill adoption but an adjusted business still contracting because US pricing was overwhelming underlying volume growth.

Q2 changed that balance.

Adjusted sales returned to growth.

Adjusted operating profit accelerated.

Management raised guidance again.

The debate has therefore shifted from **"Can commercial momentum stabilize?"** to **"Can that momentum generate durable earnings despite lower price and tougher competition?"**

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

This is a classic **beat-and-raise that failed the quality-of-growth test**.

Adjusted operating profit beat consensus by a wide margin.

Guidance moved materially higher.

The stock still fell 6%.

That tells us exactly where the market's bar sits.

Investors no longer require proof that oral Wegovy can attract patients.

They require proof that:

1. patient growth offsets price compression;
2. oral Wegovy retains leadership as Lilly scales its competing franchise;
3. Novo can replace semaglutide with differentiated next-generation assets.

The slight pill-sales miss and CagriSema data both touched those longer-duration questions.

This is therefore not best described as a "regime-suppressed" reaction.

It is a **release-specific negative reaction to forward franchise quality despite improving near-term fundamentals**.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder in repair; GARP; turnaround / self-help; setup-risk stock.**

Novo remains an exceptionally profitable global pharmaceutical franchise, but its historical scarcity premium has been damaged by competition and price normalization.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

The ADR fell **5.97% on August 4**.

The Copenhagen line then fell **4.32% on August 5**.

Reuters identified three main reasons:

* Wegovy pill sales were slightly below expectations;
* short-term positioning was demanding;
* CagriSema's latest comparison failed to establish a decisive efficacy advantage over Lilly's competing therapy.

The reaction is therefore more negative than the supplied tape suggested.

The market did not ignore the guide raise.

It explicitly judged that the guide raise did not resolve the long-duration competitive question.

GLP-1 / CONSUMPTION READ-ACROSS

Novo's patient reach continues to expand rapidly.

That is relevant to the broader consumer-discretionary debate because GLP-1 use can affect food consumption, restaurant frequency and category mix.

However, **this earnings release does not establish a causal chain from NVO prescription growth to PEP or MCD traffic declines**.

The proper PM treatment is:

**GLP-1 adoption = structural consumption-change overlay to monitor.**

PEP/MCD traffic weakness = independent consumer data.

A causal link requires additional evidence such as cohort-level spending, prescription penetration versus local restaurant consumption, or company-disclosed GLP-1 effects.

The cross-batch connection should therefore remain a **hypothesis/watch item**, not an established transmission chain.

PIPELINE / FRANCHISE-DURATION CHECK

Current franchise:

Wegovy pill prescriptions >5M cumulative.

Weekly pill prescriptions >265,000.

Obesity-care Q2 adjusted sales +16% CER.

International obesity care +37%.

Pressure points:

Wegovy pill sales slightly missed expectations.

US injectable Wegovy revenue -22% CER despite higher volumes.

Monlunabant impaired by DKK 4.0B.

ZEUS ziltivekimab Phase 3 failed its primary endpoint.

CagriSema matched rather than clearly surpassed Lilly's comparator on key recent data.

This makes the pipeline the main determinant of whether Novo eventually earns back its historical structural-growth premium.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Wegovy pill prescriptions and revenue / Why it matters: Strong TRx must convert into revenue growth despite lower prices and channel mix.

2. US GLP-1 price-volume mix / Why it matters: The central earnings question is whether access-led volume growth can outrun lower realized prices.

3. Lilly oral competition / Why it matters: Novo's current first-mover advantage in oral obesity therapy must remain durable as competitor promotion and distribution scale.

4. CagriSema and next-generation pipeline / Why it matters: Novo needs differentiated assets to preserve franchise duration beyond semaglutide.

5. International obesity growth / Why it matters: +37% CER provides the cleanest diversification away from US pricing pressure.

BOTTOM LINE

Novo Nordisk's **fundamental state improved materially in Q2**, but its valuation problem did not disappear.

Adjusted sales accelerated from -4% CER in Q1 to **+7%**.

Adjusted operating profit rose **11%**.

Obesity care grew **16%**.

FY26 guidance was raised from **-4% to -12%** to **0% to -6%** for both adjusted sales and adjusted operating profit.

That is a genuine operating improvement.

But the stock fell approximately 6% because the market is looking past 2026.

The unresolved question is:

**Can Novo translate exceptional GLP-1 patient growth into durable earnings while realized prices decline, Lilly intensifies competition and the next-generation pipeline remains less differentiated than investors once assumed?**

The state transition is therefore **Improvement**, but it is an improvement in current commercial momentum rather than a restoration of the old scarcity-premium investment case.

The rerating trigger is no longer a software-style backlog metric.

It is sustained evidence of **strong oral Wegovy monetization, acceptable US price-volume conversion and renewed pipeline differentiation**.

STATE_TRANSITION: Improvement
IMPORTANCE: ****
MARKET_REACTION: Negative
MARKET_REACTION_DETAIL: Shares fell approximately 5.5% as the cancellation of Germany's F126 frigate programme forced a €300M reduction in FY26 sales guidance, overwhelming record Q2 growth and profitability.
PM_READ: Rheinmetall's underlying rearmament cycle accelerated decisively, with H1 sales +39%, operating profit +74% and backlog at €80.5B. But the quarter also exposes the two risks that matter once demand is no longer in question: individual programme execution and the enormous working-capital burden of converting framework awards into physical output.
THEME_BUCKETS: Aerospace / Defense, Industrial Cycle, Margin / Cost Discipline, Capex / FCF Tension
THEMES: European rearmament, NATO budgets, ammunition, armoured vehicles, air defence, F126, SAFE programme, backlog conversion, capacity expansion, working capital
SEGMENT_TABLE_STATUS: Full
SOURCE_ROLE: Full note

EXEC SUMMARY

Rheinmetall reported H1 revenue of **€5.227B**, up 39%, with operating profit increasing **74% to €786M** and operating margin expanding to **15.0% from 12.1%**. The acceleration was heavily Q2-weighted: Q2 operating profit more than doubled to **€562M**, with a record **17.1% margin**. EPS from continuing operations increased to **€8.43 from €4.69**.

Demand visibility strengthened further. Rheinmetall Nomination reached **€16.2B**, +28%, while Rheinmetall Backlog rose to **€80.5B from €56.0B**. Critically, these are broader measures than conventional firm orders: Nomination includes new framework agreements, while Backlog includes expected future call-offs under existing frameworks. The €80.5B therefore provides extraordinary demand visibility, but should not be treated as equivalent to €80.5B of irrevocable contracted revenue.

The negative element was highly specific. Cancellation of the German F126 frigate programme reduced expected 2026 Naval Systems revenue by up to **€300M**, forcing group sales guidance down from €14.0-14.5B to **€13.7-14.2B**. The approximately **19% operating-margin target was maintained**, indicating that the setback affects programme revenue rather than the broader profitability thesis.

Official backlog definitions are crucial: Rheinmetall says Nomination includes both traditional orders and framework agreements, while Backlog also includes expected future call-offs from frameworks.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

H1 sales increased from €3.749B to **€5.227B**, a 39% increase.

Q1 sales were €1.938B.

That implies approximately **€3.289B of Q2 sales**, nearly 69% above the implied Q2 2025 base.

The acceleration is exactly what management had signalled after Q1, when timing delays shifted deliveries into Q2.

Germany represented 38% of H1 group sales, up 5 percentage points.

International markets represented 62%.

Consensus:

Reliable H1/Q2 group consensus was not confirmed.

The production archive should therefore avoid manufacturing a beat/miss against an unverified Street number.

The observable surprise was instead the **combination of record Q2 margin and a negative guidance revision caused by F126**.

EPS / Earnings:

EPS from continuing operations increased to **€8.43** from €4.69.

Group operating result increased 74% to €786M.

Q2 operating result rose **115% to €562M**.

Profitability / Margins:

H1 operating margin increased to **15.0% from 12.1%**.

Q2 margin reached **17.1%**, a company record.

Management attributes the disproportionate profit increase to:

* favourable product mix;
* considerably higher production volumes;
* operating leverage.

The FY26 margin target remains approximately **19%** despite the sales-guide reduction.

Key KPI:

The defining KPI is not absolute backlog alone.

It is **backlog conversion into revenue, margin and cash**.

Rheinmetall has already established extraordinary demand visibility.

The investment question is now whether it can build inventory, factories, labour and supplier capacity quickly enough to convert that demand without permanently consuming disproportionate cash.

That makes the hierarchy:

**Nomination / frameworks → firm call-offs → production → sales → operating margin → operating FCF.**

Guidance headline:

FY26 sales guidance is now:

**€13.7B-€14.2B**

versus:

**€14.0B-€14.5B previously.**

Organic sales growth remains expected at **28%-31%**.

Operating margin remains expected around **19%**.

The entire €300M reduction is linked to the F126 frigate programme cancellation.

Capital allocation:

The priority remains industrial capacity expansion rather than aggressive shareholder distribution.

Operating FCF was **-€1.616B**, deteriorating by €985M YoY.

Management attributes the cash use to:

* changed timing of customer advance payments;
* receivables following heavy quarter-end invoicing;
* inventory required for future production;
* continued high investment activity.

This is economically consistent with an extreme production ramp, but it prevents backlog growth from being treated as automatically equivalent to high-quality near-term cash generation.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Clean operationally.

Adjustment issue:

No material non-operating gain, fair-value mark, tax benefit or restructuring item was identified that alters the H1 operating interpretation.

The main quality issue is **cash conversion rather than accounting conversion**.

Operating profit increased by €333M.

Operating FCF deteriorated by €985M.

That divergence comes primarily from working capital and investment rather than earnings adjustments.

PM interpretation:

The income statement is unusually clean for such a large industrial ramp.

The correct concern is not whether reported profit is artificial.

It is whether the capital intensity required to deliver the backlog ultimately consumes more cash than the current valuation assumes.

Vehicle Systems generated €275M of operating profit, Weapon & Ammunition €417M, Air Defence €76M, Digital Systems €63M and newly consolidated Naval Systems €33M.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The H1 print confirms that European rearmament has moved well beyond the budget-announcement stage.

The causal chain is now observable:

**higher NATO / national defence budgets → procurement awards and framework agreements → Rheinmetall Nomination / Backlog → inventory and factory expansion → revenue growth → margin leverage → temporary cash absorption.**

This is the appropriate **Defense Capex Overlay**.

The direction matters.

Rheinmetall backlog does not cause NATO budget allocation.

Political and defence-budget decisions create procurement demand, which eventually becomes framework awards and orders in Rheinmetall's book.

Capacity is now the bottleneck.

Weapon & Ammunition alone invested approximately **€184M in H1** in transformation and capacity, including:

* UK barrel-production expansion;
* the new Lithuania facility;
* Nitrochemie Aschau powder capacity.

The company is spending ahead of revenue because the policy-driven demand wall is already visible.

Revision direction: **Downward revisions.**

FY26 group sales estimates decline by approximately €300M following F126.

Margin assumptions should remain broadly unchanged.

Longer-duration land, ammunition, air-defence and digital estimates remain supported by growing programme visibility.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

Rheinmetall is the European defence analogue of the AI-capex conversion framework.

The demand debate is largely finished.

An €80.5B backlog against a current annual sales base around €10B is more visibility than the company can physically monetize in the near term.

But the instrument requires more care than a conventional GE/CAT backlog.

Rheinmetall explicitly includes **expected framework call-offs**.

Its Nomination measure similarly includes both firm traditional orders and newly signed framework agreement volumes.

Therefore:

**€80.5B Backlog ≠ €80.5B of equally hard contracted revenue.**

That qualification is important, particularly when comparing Rheinmetall to BAE, GE Aerospace or Caterpillar.

The F126 event provides a useful demonstration of the distinction.

A large programme that had been embedded in the naval growth path disappeared.

The broader demand cycle did not change.

But a specific piece of expected revenue did.

That is precisely the kind of risk that remains even when sovereign defence budgets are structurally rising.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The strongest positive signal is the **Q2 margin of 17.1%**.

Backlog growth alone can remain theoretical for years.

Margin expansion proves that backlog is already converting into economically attractive production.

The second signal is breadth.

All major defence segments are growing revenue and improving margins.

The third is Air Defence.

Sales +62%, Nomination +517% and Backlog +80% show that European short-range air-defence procurement is moving particularly rapidly.

The strongest negative signal is operating FCF.

-€1.616B is not a cosmetic issue.

It reflects the balance-sheet cost of moving from a lower-volume contractor to a mass-production defence industrial.

The second negative is F126.

It demonstrates that programme concentration and political procurement decisions remain relevant even inside a secular rearmament supercycle.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q1 had looked optically weak because sales of €1.94B missed expectations and deliveries shifted into Q2.

Management argued that this was phasing rather than weakening demand.

Q2 validated that explanation.

Revenue accelerated sharply and operating margin reached a record.

The new debate is therefore no longer whether delayed Q1 production will convert.

It is whether the enormous production ramp can deliver **19% margins and normalized cash conversion simultaneously**.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

Fundamentally, Rheinmetall delivered an exceptional operating quarter.

Yet the stock fell 5.5%.

Unlike AMAT's setup-driven fade, this decline contained genuine new negative information.

F126 was cancelled.

FY revenue guidance fell €300M.

The market therefore repriced **programme quality**, not European defence demand.

That distinction is critical.

The print does not invalidate the European rearmament thesis.

It demonstrates that an extraordinary top-down demand backdrop does not eliminate bottom-up contract risk.

The most useful valuation framework is therefore:

**budget visibility × award quality × production capacity × margin conversion × cash conversion.**

Rheinmetall currently scores exceptionally on the first four.

Cash conversion is the weak link.

The stock rerates higher if:

* Q3/Q4 sales remain at the new Q2 cadence;
* operating margin approaches the 19% FY target;
* operating FCF begins reversing sharply as customer advance payments and inventory normalize.

The thesis weakens if:

* framework call-offs are delayed;
* additional large programmes are cancelled or pushed out;
* working-capital consumption persists despite higher deliveries.

Stock identity:

**Secular growth at a cyclical multiple; expensive but deliverable; crowded long; setup-risk stock.**

The secular demand environment is unusually durable.

The equity remains vulnerable because expectations increasingly require flawless execution against that demand.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

Rheinmetall shares fell approximately **5.5%** following the result.

Reuters linked the move directly to the F126-driven reduction in FY sales guidance.

The read-across for the broader European defence cohort remains strongly constructive.

Vehicle Systems:
backlog +41%.

Weapon & Ammunition:
backlog +17%.

Air Defence:
backlog +80%.

Digital Systems:
backlog sharply higher.

The specific Naval setback therefore does not represent a reversal in land, ammunition, air-defence or digital procurement.

For **BAE / Saab / Thales / Leonardo**, the important lesson is that backlog quantity is insufficient.

The market increasingly needs:

**backlog composition + contractual firmness + production capacity + cash conversion.**

DEFENSE CAPEX OVERLAY

Status: **Activated.**

Stage 1 — Government commitment:
European/NATO defence budgets are structurally increasing.

Stage 2 — Procurement:
SAFE and national programmes translate budgets into tenders, orders and frameworks.

Stage 3 — Contractor visibility:
Rheinmetall Nomination €16.2B.
Rheinmetall Backlog €80.5B.

Stage 4 — Capacity:
Ammunition, powder, vehicle and air-defence production capacity is expanding.

Stage 5 — P&L conversion:
H1 sales +39%.
Q2 operating profit +115%.
Q2 margin 17.1%.

Stage 6 — Cash conversion:
Operating FCF **-€1.616B**.

The first five stages are clearly functioning.

Stage six is the current constraint.

That makes Rheinmetall the cleanest evidence so far that **European defence spending is converting into industrial earnings, but the conversion requires substantial balance-sheet financing before cash catches up.**

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Operating FCF / Why it matters: A sharp H2 recovery is required to demonstrate that H1 cash burn is phasing rather than structurally higher capital intensity.

2. Framework call-offs / Why it matters: Backlog includes expected framework volumes, so actual call-offs determine the quality of the headline €80.5B instrument.

3. F126 replacement opportunities / Why it matters: New naval awards could offset the €300M 2026 revenue loss and restore confidence in the naval growth plan.

4. Weapon & Ammunition margin / Why it matters: 23.7% is the strongest economics in the group and central to the 19% consolidated FY target.

5. Air Defence conversion / Why it matters: Nomination +517% must translate into production without creating a new capacity bottleneck.

BOTTOM LINE

Rheinmetall delivered an **exceptional operating improvement accompanied by a useful reminder that sovereign backlog is not risk-free**.

H1 sales rose 39%.

Operating profit rose 74%.

Q2 margin reached a record 17.1%.

Backlog reached €80.5B.

The European rearmament cycle is clearly converting into Rheinmetall's P&L.

But two qualifications now matter.

First, the €80.5B backlog includes expected framework call-offs and is therefore not equivalent to €80.5B of uniformly firm contracts.

Second, production acceleration required **€1.616B of H1 operating cash absorption**.

F126 then demonstrated the remaining programme-level risk by removing €300M from FY sales guidance and driving a 5.5% share-price decline.

The correct state is therefore **Improvement**, but not a frictionless beat-and-raise.

The defence demand thesis is stronger.

The investment debate has moved downstream to:

**contract quality → production execution → cash conversion.**

EXEC SUMMARY

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

H1 revenue reached **€21.226B**.

Organic growth was **14.0%**.

Reported growth was **9.8%**.

The official bridge was approximately:

**+14.0% organic**
**-3.9% FX**
**+0.1% scope**

This corrects the preliminary tape's approximately +12.5% reported-growth figure.

Q2 revenue was €11.46B.

Organic growth accelerated to 16.5%.

Reported growth was 14.5%.

Scope effects were immaterial, so the Q2 organic/reported gap was primarily FX.

Consensus:

H1 revenue of €21.2B exceeded approximately €20.6B consensus.

Adjusted EBITA of €4.09B exceeded approximately €3.8B.

Organic growth also materially exceeded the market bar.

EPS / Earnings:

Net income reached approximately **€2.5B**, up 30%.

The core earnings measure remains adjusted EBITA rather than EPS.

Adjusted EBITA increased to **€4.093B** from €3.510B reported one year earlier.

Profitability / Margins:

Adjusted EBITA margin increased to **19.3% from 18.2%**.

Energy Management:

Adjusted EBITA €3.957B.

Margin **22.4%**, versus 21.5%.

Industrial Automation:

Adjusted EBITA €501M.

Margin **14.0%**, versus 13.7%.

Gross margin reached approximately 42.5%.

Industrial productivity, product pricing and volume leverage more than offset raw-material inflation, tariff costs and negative mix in Energy Management.

Key KPI:

The most important structural KPI is **Systems growth at +28%**.

Products still represent 47% of quarterly revenue and grew 13%.

But Systems now represent 35% and are growing more than twice as fast.

Software & Services represent the remaining 18% and grew 6%.

That changes the quality of Schneider's AI exposure.

It is not simply selling more circuit breakers or UPS units.

Customers are purchasing:

prefabricated data-center infrastructure;
cooling;
power distribution;
three-phase UPS;
engineering;
digital integration.

That increases Schneider's content per data-center project.

Guidance headline:

Old FY26:

Organic revenue growth **7%-10%**.

Adjusted EBITA margin **19.1%-19.4%**.

Organic adjusted EBITA growth **10%-15%**.

New FY26:

Organic revenue growth **10%-13%**.

Adjusted EBITA margin **19.4%-19.7%**.

Organic adjusted EBITA growth **14%-19%**.

This is a genuine upward revision.

But H1 organic growth is already 14%, meaning the new full-year range deliberately embeds H2 moderation.

Capital allocation:

FCF reached approximately **€1.6B**, up 244%.

R&D investment remained approximately 5.8% of revenue, around €1.2B in H1.

Schneider is also increasing its AI/software exposure through the proposed Cognite and AiDASH acquisitions.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Clean.

Adjustment issue:

No material one-time gain, fair-value mark, tax benefit or impairment materially distorts the operating quarter.

The main reconciliation is FX.

Reported H1 revenue growth of 9.8% materially understates organic growth of 14.0%.

PM interpretation:

Unlike several U.S. AI names, Schneider does not require a large GAAP/non-GAAP normalization to understand the quarter.

The operating evidence is unusually direct:

revenue growth;
systems mix;
margin expansion;
FCF;
guide raise.

The main external distortion is currency translation.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

Schneider is the **rack-to-grid interior** of the AI-power build.

Siemens Energy sells many of the assets required to create and move incremental megawatts:

gas turbines;
large transformers;
transmission infrastructure.

Schneider increasingly sells the integrated infrastructure required to receive and distribute that power inside the data-center campus:

medium- and low-voltage distribution;
prefabricated power systems;
cooling;
UPS;
software;
monitoring;
lifecycle services.

The Systems mix shift therefore matters more than raw revenue growth.

A move from standalone Products toward Systems increases:

project wallet share;
engineering content;
switching costs;
potential recurring service attachment.

This is the physical-economy equivalent of software moving from point products toward platforms.

Revision direction: **Upward revisions.**

Revenue estimates rise.

Adjusted EBITA rises.

Margin estimates rise.

The remaining debate is how much H2 moderation should be embedded after an exceptional H1.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

Schneider and Siemens Energy together make the European AI-power chain unusually clear.

**Siemens Energy:** generation + transmission scarcity.

**Schneider:** data-center electrical distribution + cooling + integrated systems.

Both are experiencing high demand.

But the earnings instruments are different.

Siemens Energy provides a **€162B hard backlog instrument** and years of turbine/grid visibility.

Schneider provides **current revenue acceleration, systems mix and a formal guide raise**, but does not disclose a directly comparable absolute backlog number in the accessible H1 summary.

That distinction is important.

Schneider's evidence is nearer-term and income-statement based.

Siemens Energy's is longer-duration and order-book based.

The July 30 market reaction demonstrates how much the pricing regime values the former when it arrives with a new guide.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The highest-quality signal is **Systems +28%**.

It shows that the data-center cycle is raising Schneider's content per project rather than merely increasing units.

The second is North America +23.1%.

This directly matches hyperscaler capex acceleration.

The third is the simultaneous recovery in Industrial Automation.

The bull case therefore no longer requires data centers to carry every division.

The main caution is H2 arithmetic.

With H1 at +14% organic and FY guidance at 10%-13%, the company explicitly assumes deceleration.

That is prudent, but it creates a high Q3/Q4 bar for continued estimate upgrades.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

At FY25 and Q1, Schneider was already recognized as a major data-center electrification beneficiary.

The remaining debate was whether the AI demand burst was too concentrated and whether the rest of Industrial Automation remained cyclical.

H1 improved both sides of that debate.

Data-center growth accelerated further.

Industrial Automation also reaccelerated.

The company therefore moved from a narrow AI proxy toward a broader electrification-and-automation growth instrument.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

This is almost a textbook example of the season's **step-change rule**.

The stock entered with high expectations.

But the company delivered:

record Q2 revenue;
organic growth well above consensus;
a 120bp organic margin improvement;
very strong FCF;
a significant revenue-guide raise;
a significant EBITA-guide raise.

Shares responded **+10.83% on July 30** and **+1.76% on July 31**.

So positioning did not dominate.

The information content was large enough to clear the elevated bar.

This directly contrasts with Siemens Energy.

Siemens delivered superb results but merely reaffirmed the guide that had already been raised after H1.

Schneider delivered a new revision event.

The reaction dispersion is therefore consistent with the wider earnings-season rule:

**excellent print + held guide = limited rerating.**

**excellent print + material guide raise = rerating.**

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder; secular growth at a cyclical multiple; beat-and-raise machine; crowded long.**

The valuation is demanding, but Q2 supplied the evidence required to support it.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

July 29 close: **€256.80**.

July 30 close: **€284.60**, +10.83%.

July 31 close: **€289.60**, +1.76%.

That is roughly a **12.8% two-session rerating**.

The read-across is constructive for:

Siemens Energy;
GE Vernova;
Vertiv;
Eaton;
ABB;
Legrand;
data-center cooling;
switchgear;
transformers;
UPS and power-distribution suppliers.

The most important cross-check is that growth is not confined to North America.

China & East Asia grew **19.7% organic**, supported by data centers, semiconductor facilities and Southeast Asian projects.

AI POWER / RACK-TO-GRID CHECK

External power:

Siemens Energy gas/grid backlog.

Utility generation.

Transmission.

Transformers.

Campus layer:

Schneider prefabricated systems.

Cooling.

Medium/low-voltage distribution.

UPS.

Software.

Rack layer:

Compute and networking.

The Q2 Schneider tape therefore closes the infrastructure chain between utility electricity and installed compute.

Systems +28% is the key datapoint because it indicates customers increasingly buy the whole integrated electrical architecture.

The remaining overbuild risk is downstream.

If hyperscaler data-center starts slow, Systems should decelerate before long-lived utility backlogs unwind.

That makes Schneider a potentially faster cycle indicator than Siemens Energy.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Systems growth / Why it matters: Sustained >20% growth confirms increasing content per data-center project.

2. Data-center growth / Why it matters: Current triple-digit growth cannot persist indefinitely; the deceleration path matters more than the absolute level.

3. Energy Management margin / Why it matters: 22.4% demonstrates operating leverage despite tariffs and raw-material inflation.

4. Industrial Automation / Why it matters: Continued double-digit recovery would broaden the thesis beyond AI capex.

5. H2 organic growth / Why it matters: FY guidance mathematically embeds moderation from H1's 14%; beating through that assumption would drive another revision cycle.

BOTTOM LINE

Schneider Electric delivered a **decisive improvement and a genuine rerating event**.

Q2 revenue grew **16.5% organically**.

Systems grew **28%**.

North America grew **23%**.

Adjusted EBITA margin reached **19.3%** in H1.

FCF increased sharply.

Management then raised both revenue and EBITA guidance.

The stock responded almost **13% over two sessions**.

The strategic signal is larger than the headline beat:

**AI data-center spending is moving from isolated electrical components toward integrated rack-to-grid infrastructure, and Schneider's revenue mix is moving with it.**

That increases wallet share and strengthens the durability of the data-center exposure.

The principal forward risk is no longer whether demand exists.

It is whether today's exceptional data-center growth decelerates faster than Schneider's broader electrification and automation businesses can absorb.

For now, the state is **Improvement — decisive and paid.**

EXEC SUMMARY

Siemens Energy delivered its strongest quarter to date. Orders reached a record **€17.93B**, revenue reached **€11.45B**, up 18.5% comparable, and profit before special items more than tripled to **€1.623B**. Net income rose 70% to €1.188B and basic EPS increased to €1.28 from €0.71.

Gas Services backlog reached roughly €73B and Grid Technologies roughly €51B. U.S. data centers, Middle Eastern generation projects and transmission investment remain the principal demand drivers.

The qualitative step-change was Siemens Gamesa. The wind business delivered a positive quarterly operating result for the first time since fiscal 2022 and remains on track for FY26 break-even. That removes one of the largest offsets to the extraordinarily strong Gas Services and Grid franchises.

That can normalize when order growth eventually slows.

Reported earnings quality:

Clean.

The current quarter therefore actually faces an unfavorable reported comparison from below-the-line structural items.

PM interpretation:

There is no material accounting-quality objection to the Q3 improvement.

The relevant FCF quality question is instead **working-capital duration**.

Customer advances improve today's cash conversion but can reverse when the order cycle normalizes.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

Siemens Energy is now one of the clearest **hard-evidence instruments** in the AI-power chain.

The disclosed backlog is fundamentally different from management commentary about future electricity demand.

Customers have already committed to equipment and project schedules extending several years.

The gas-turbine backlog is roughly **69GW**, against annual delivery capability of only around 15-16GW according to the supplied call tape.

That creates several years of embedded visibility even before additional orders.

Grid equipment displays the same scarcity dynamics.

Transformer lead times approaching two years in parts of Europe and a €51B Grid Technologies backlog demonstrate that the bottleneck has migrated from generating AI demand to physically delivering megawatts.

The key risk is therefore no longer order scarcity.

It is **cycle duration and future working-capital reversal**.

Revision direction: **No material consensus change.**

Q3 did not raise formal guidance again.

But execution should push estimates toward the upper end of the existing margin range.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

This quarter closes another link in the AI physical chain.

The season already showed:

hyperscaler contracted demand;
semiconductor capex;
WFE shipments;
data-center construction;
on-site generation;
utility load.

Siemens Energy now shows the upstream power-equipment response.

U.S. data centers are directly contributing to gas-turbine orders.

Grid projects are driving transformer demand.

Customers are paying reservation fees to secure future manufacturing slots.

That is stronger evidence than generic AI-power commentary.

The other important feature is **Gamesa**.

Until now, Siemens Energy's valuation contained two very different companies:

an extraordinary scarcity asset in Gas/Grid;

and a wind restructuring problem consuming part of those economics.

Q3 is the first evidence that both sides of that equation can contribute positively at the same time.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The highest-quality signal is the combination of:

**1.57x group book-to-bill + record revenue.**

Demand is still outrunning delivery even as capacity expands.

The second is nearly 20% Grid margin.

The third is Gamesa profitability.

The main caution is that FCF is partially advance-payment driven.

The bearish cycle-peak argument therefore should not focus on current orders—the Q3 data clearly reject an immediate rollover.

It should focus on what happens to FCF when:

order growth normalizes;
reservation payments stabilize;
capacity catches up.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q2 had already forced a major guidance raise after record orders.

Q3 did not provide another guide increase.

Instead, it validated the raised trajectory through record revenue, better margins and positive wind profitability.

The debate has shifted from **"Is the AI/grid power cycle real?"** to **"How long can scarcity margins and advance-payment cash conversion persist?"**

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

The actual market reaction is cleaner than the scout implied.

ENR closed August 5 at **€150.86**, essentially unchanged from August 4, despite trading as low as €147.68 intraday.

It then rose **1.87% on August 6**.

This is not a meaningful fundamental rejection.

The better interpretation is:

**record print → initial expectations liquidation → evidence absorbed → modest positive follow-through.**

Unlike Schneider, Siemens did not deliver a new guide raise in the quarter.

That explains much of the reaction difference.

The stock already embodied a strong cycle assumption, so execution against a previously raised guide generated less incremental information.

The re-rating case now requires **duration**, not another single-quarter beat.

Stock identity:

**Secular growth at a cyclical multiple; turnaround; crowded long; expensive but deliverable.**

The business has transitioned from restructuring toward scarcity-driven industrial growth.

The residual valuation question is how much of today's economics are mid-cycle versus peak-cycle.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

Print-day close: essentially flat.

Next session: **+1.87%**.

The read-across is highly constructive for:

GE Vernova;
Mitsubishi Heavy;
transformer manufacturers;
grid equipment;
data-center generation;
gas-turbine service supply chains.

Gas Services orders rose approximately 62% comparable and Grid orders about 28%.

The result strongly supports the VST/CAT conclusion that electricity infrastructure is becoming one of the terminal physical constraints on AI deployment.

AI POWER / CONSTRAINT CHECK

Demand side:

Hyperscalers continue raising compute investment.

VST reports record electricity peaks and data-center PPAs.

CAT reports data-center-linked power-generation demand.

Supply side:

Siemens Energy gas backlog: approximately **69GW**.

Gas Services backlog: **€73B**.

Grid Technologies backlog: **€51B**.

Current turbine delivery capacity: approximately 15-16GW annually per the supplied call tape.

The physical implication is straightforward:

**power-equipment capacity is substantially behind booked demand.**

The principal eventual bearish trigger is therefore not a modest quarterly order slowdown.

It is backlog cancellation, price compression or manufacturing capacity catching demand materially faster than expected.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Gas turbine orders / Why it matters: Sustained high intake extends scarcity duration; normalization below roughly 40GW annualized would indicate the cycle is maturing.

2. Grid Technologies margin / Why it matters: Maintaining approximately 19%-20% shows scarcity economics surviving the capacity ramp.

3. Siemens Gamesa profitability / Why it matters: A return to losses would weaken the new group earnings-quality regime.

4. Customer advances / Why it matters: They are a material contributor to exceptional FCF and can reverse when order growth moderates.

5. Capacity expansion / Why it matters: More supply monetizes backlog but eventually reduces scarcity pricing.

BOTTOM LINE

Siemens Energy delivered a **decisive improvement**.

Orders, revenue, profit and backlog all reached records.

Gas and Grid are converting scarcity into high-teens margins.

Siemens Gamesa turned profitable for the first time since FY2022.

FCF accelerated dramatically.

The AI-power thesis is therefore no longer dependent on anticipated electricity demand.

It is visible in **booked turbines, transformer orders, customer advances and delivered margins**.

The remaining bear case is more sophisticated:

**today's economics may represent the peak of a capacity-constrained cycle, and part of today's cash generation is being funded by unusually strong advance payments.**

Q3 provides no evidence that the cycle has already peaked.

The next arbitration is duration.

<<ENR.DE_EARNINGS_END>>
The TotalEnergies audit confirms the key correction to the original scout: **Q2 was not an LNG-diversion windfall.** Integrated LNG earnings fell **22% YoY to $807M** on weak gas trading, while **E&P rose 64% to $3.23B** and **Refining & Chemicals surged to $1.80B**. Middle East disruption removed roughly **210 kboe/d** of production, yet higher liquids prices and exceptional refining margins more than compensated at group level.  ([Reuters][1])

MARKET_REACTION: Positive
MARKET_REACTION_DETAIL: Paris shares rose 2.54% on July 23 to €76.18, while the NYSE ADR rose 1.55%; the market rewarded the integrated portfolio's ability to convert Middle East disruption into higher upstream and refining earnings despite lost physical production and weak LNG trading.
PM_READ: TotalEnergies demonstrated that geopolitical disruption is two-sided for an integrated major: Hormuz-related outages materially reduced production, but the resulting crude/product dislocation drove substantially higher upstream realizations and exceptional refining economics. LNG was the weak leg, not the earnings driver.
THEME_BUCKETS: Energy / Commodities, Margin / Cost Discipline, FX / Macro Translation

EXEC SUMMARY

TotalEnergies reported Q2 revenue from sales of **$57.10B**, up 28% YoY, adjusted net income of **$6.03B**, up roughly 68% YoY and 12% QoQ, and adjusted diluted EPS of **$2.68**. Adjusted EBITDA reached approximately $13.2B and CFFO increased to **$9.80B**. The $6.0B adjusted earnings result was broadly in line with market expectations.

The quarter's economic composition matters more than the headline. Hydrocarbon production fell 4% YoY to **2.395 Mboe/d**, including an approximately **210 kboe/d** Middle East conflict impact. Excluding the conflict, production would have increased more than 4%. Exploration & Production adjusted net operating income rose to **$3.23B**, while Refining & Chemicals reached **$1.80B** as crude and product-market disruptions produced unusually strong refining and oil-trading economics.

Integrated LNG moved in the opposite direction. Adjusted net operating income fell to **$807M**, down sharply QoQ and 22% YoY, because gas trading underperformed in a broadly flat-to-bearish European market. Q2 therefore validates an **integrated-major shock-absorption thesis**, not a simple LNG-diversion thesis.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 revenue from sales reached **$57.097B**, versus $44.676B one year earlier.

Gross sales before excise taxes were $61.771B.

The revenue increase primarily reflects the sharp rise in oil and refined-product pricing associated with the Middle East disruption.

Consensus:

Adjusted net income of $6.03B was broadly in line with the approximately $6.0B market expectation.

Reliable revenue and adjusted EPS consensus were not independently confirmed and are not inserted.

EPS / Earnings:

Reported net income attributable to TotalEnergies shareholders was **$5.438B**.

Adjusted net income was **$6.027B**.

Adjusted diluted EPS was **$2.68**, versus $2.45 in Q1.

H1 adjusted net income reached $11.42B.

Profitability / Margins:

Adjusted net operating income from business segments reached **$6.871B**, versus $6.300B in Q1.

The improvement was driven by:

* substantially higher oil prices;
* very strong refining and petrochemical margins;
* strong crude-oil and petroleum-products trading.

The offset was Integrated LNG, where gas-trading performance deteriorated materially.

Key KPI:

The defining KPI for this quarter is **earnings captured per unit of physical disruption**.

Total production fell because of the conflict.

Yet cash flow and adjusted earnings increased.

That is precisely what an integrated portfolio is designed to do:

**lost production → higher commodity price → higher remaining upstream realizations → product scarcity → higher refining margins → trading opportunity.**

The offset was not perfect and was not uniform across segments.

LNG proves that distinction.

Guidance headline:

For Q3:

* oil prices were above $80/b at the start of the quarter;
* European refining margins remained historically elevated;
* European gas forwards were around $16-$20/MMBtu;
* TotalEnergies expects average LNG selling prices above **$11.5/MMBtu**;
* ex-Middle East conflict, production is expected to grow approximately **3% YoY**;
* conflict-related production exposure remains approximately **5%-10% of company production**, depending on export access through Hormuz;
* refinery utilization is expected at **80%-85%**;
* SATORP is expected to return to full capacity around the end of Q3.

Capital allocation:

Q2 net investments were **$3.4B**.

H1 net investments reached **$7.9B**, consistent with FY guidance of approximately **$15B**.

TotalEnergies repurchased **$1.5B** of shares in Q2.

The Board authorized another **$1.5B** for Q3.

The second interim dividend was raised **5.9% to €0.90/share**.

Net debt declined by approximately **$3.3B**, taking gearing to **13.1%**.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed but transparently reconciled.

Adjustment issue:

Reported net income was $5.438B versus adjusted net income of $6.027B.

The approximately **$589M net adjustment** principally consisted of:

* approximately $268M of special items;
* approximately $290M after-tax FIFO inventory effects;
* approximately $31M of fair-value effects.

Special items included restructuring and other non-recurring items.

There was no large Q2 impairment.

The Middle East conflict had also not created an impairment indicator as of June 30.

PM interpretation:

Adjusted results are the better operating measure because TotalEnergies' FIFO accounting can materially distort refining earnings during periods of rapidly changing commodity prices.

This is particularly important in Q2 because oil prices rose sharply during the conflict and then declined toward quarter-end.

The adjusted replacement-cost framework therefore gives a cleaner view of downstream margin capture.

The underlying earnings strength remains genuine after the accounting bridge.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The quarter materially changes the way the Middle East exposure should be modeled.

The original one-directional framework was:

**Hormuz disruption → LNG diversion → higher LNG earnings.**

The actual Q2 result was:

**Hormuz / regional disruption**
→ **physical production loss**
→ **higher crude and refined-product prices**
→ **higher upstream realization on available barrels**
→ **exceptional refining margins**
→ **strong oil-trading opportunity**
→ **higher group earnings**

while simultaneously:

**European gas market softness**
→ **weak gas trading**
→ **lower Integrated LNG earnings.**

That is a much more useful framework for integrated majors.

TotalEnergies also disclosed explicit commodity sensitivities for 2026:

A +/-$10/b move in average liquids prices corresponds to approximately **+/-$2.3B of adjusted net operating income** and approximately **+/-$2.8B of cash flow**.

A +/-$1/b move in the European Refining Margin Marker corresponds to approximately **+/-$0.3B of adjusted NOI** and approximately **+/-$0.4B of cash flow**.

Those sensitivities are more useful instruments than attempting to manufacture a backlog analogue for an oil major.

Revision direction: Mix-shift revisions.

Upstream and refining assumptions move higher.

Integrated LNG trading assumptions move lower.

Aggregate FY earnings remain heavily commodity- and conflict-dependent rather than governed by a conventional company EPS guide.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

TotalEnergies is the first European major to demonstrate how the Middle East shock actually moves through an integrated energy P&L.

The result is not simply:

**more geopolitical risk = more profit.**

The company lost roughly **210 kboe/d** of production during Q2.

Middle East and North Africa production fell from 850 to 671 kboe/d YoY.

Qatar, Iraq and UAE operations were all disrupted.

Some produced barrels could not even be lifted because of Strait access.

That is a real economic cost.

But the same shock created:

higher oil realizations;
fuel scarcity;
record-like refining margins;
strong oil-trading conditions.

Those offsets were larger than the physical production loss.

The portfolio therefore worked exactly as an integrated-major portfolio should.

The surprise is that LNG did **not** participate.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The highest-quality positive signal is **Refining & Chemicals $1.8B adjusted NOI**.

A 362% YoY increase shows that product scarcity was at least as important as upstream oil-price beta.

The second is E&P.

Despite physical disruption, adjusted NOI increased materially because the surviving barrels were worth much more.

The third is balance-sheet conversion.

Gearing fell to 13.1% while dividends and buybacks continued.

The negative signal is Integrated LNG.

The segment moved from $1.318B in Q1 to $807M in Q2 despite a geopolitical environment that superficially looked ideal for an LNG trader.

That demonstrates why **volatility alone does not guarantee trading profits**.

Positioning, regional price spreads and the quality of trading execution matter.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q1 already showed substantial energy-price sensitivity following the initial Middle East disruption.

The outstanding question was whether Q2 would produce a simple across-the-board windfall as the conflict intensified.

It did not.

Q2 showed much more granular economics:

upstream strong;
refining exceptional;
oil trading strong;
LNG trading weak;
physical Middle East production impaired.

The thesis therefore moves from simple commodity beta toward **portfolio-offset quality**.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

Paris shares rose from €74.29 to **€76.18**, a **2.54%** gain on July 23.

The NYSE ADR gained approximately **1.55%** in its corresponding session.

The different percentage moves reflect listing hours and FX rather than different fundamental interpretations.

The market rewarded a result that was essentially in line at adjusted net income because the **composition was stronger than feared**.

Investors received:

high upstream cash conversion;
exceptional downstream profitability;
rapid deleveraging;
continued buybacks;
higher dividend.

The failure of Integrated LNG to participate capped the quality of the beat.

This therefore was not a classic earnings-surprise rerating.

It was a **portfolio-resilience validation**.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder; cyclical value; yield / income stock.**

The investment case combines cyclical commodity exposure with a structurally diversified cash-return model.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

TotalEnergies closed **+2.54% at €76.18** on July 23.

The reaction was fundamentally positive.

The most important read-across to Shell is **not** that LNG must have produced a windfall.

TotalEnergies proves the opposite.

Instead, Shell should be evaluated on whether its own portfolio flexibility and trading platform allowed it to monetize the same dislocation better.

The broader read-across is:

**Upstream:** positive commodity-price beta.

**Refiners:** very strong product-margin beta.

**LNG traders:** outcome depends on portfolio positioning, not just volatility.

**Middle East producers:** physical outage/export risk remains significant.

This is the appropriate cross-company framework for the quarter.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Strait of Hormuz export access / Why it matters: TotalEnergies estimates 5%-10% of group production remains exposed depending on effective export capability.

2. Middle East production normalization / Why it matters: Recovered volumes would add directly to already-elevated upstream realizations.

3. Refining margins / Why it matters: Q2's $1.8B downstream result depends on historically exceptional product scarcity and cannot be extrapolated indefinitely.

4. Integrated LNG trading / Why it matters: A rebound from $807M would broaden earnings quality; persistent weakness would challenge the supposed portfolio-trading advantage.

5. SATORP / Why it matters: Return to full capacity at the end of Q3 would restore physical refining volume while margins remain elevated.

BOTTOM LINE

TotalEnergies delivered a **strong integrated-major shock-absorption print**.

Adjusted net income reached **$6.03B**.

CFFO reached **$9.80B**.

E&P earnings rose sharply.

Refining & Chemicals delivered **$1.80B** of adjusted earnings.

Gearing fell to **13.1%**.

Shareholder returns continued.

But those results were achieved despite losing approximately **210 kboe/d** of Middle East production.

And critically, **Integrated LNG earnings fell 22% YoY**.

The correct state transition is therefore **Improvement**, but the source of improvement is very specific:

**higher upstream realizations + exceptional refining/product margins + strong oil trading > physical production losses + weak LNG trading.**

That changes the Middle East overlay for the entire energy cohort.

The key question for Shell is no longer:

**"Did LNG benefit from Hormuz?"**

It is:

**"Did Shell's integrated LNG/trading platform monetize the same dislocation more effectively than TotalEnergies?"**

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 revenue was **€9.878B**.

Reported growth was 9%.

Constant-currency growth was 11%.

Cloud revenue reached **€6.281B**, up 24% cc.

Cloud ERP Suite revenue reached **€5.525B**, up 27% cc.

Software-license revenue fell 32% to €131M.

Software-support revenue declined 8% to €2.439B.

The mix shift is therefore continuing rapidly from perpetual software toward cloud subscription revenue.

Consensus:

Reliable local-line consensus for all headline items was not independently confirmed.

The market's dominant positive surprise was current cloud backlog at **+26% cc**.

Reuters characterized the operating-profit outlook trim as acquisition-related rather than an organic deterioration.

EPS / Earnings:

IFRS profit after tax reached **€2.209B**, up 26%.

IFRS basic EPS was **€1.89**, up 30%.

Non-IFRS profit after tax was **€1.828B**, up 5%.

Non-IFRS basic EPS was **€1.59**, up 6%.

Profitability / Margins:

IFRS operating profit was **€2.643B**, up 8%.

Non-IFRS operating profit was **€2.743B**, up 7% and 9% cc.

Non-IFRS operating margin was **27.8%**, versus 28.5% one year earlier.

Non-IFRS cloud gross margin declined 60bp to **74.6%**.

Management attributes the sequential moderation in operating-profit growth to:

* slower sequential cloud and total-revenue growth;
* unusually low Q1 SBC;
* accelerated R&D investment;
* dilution from Reltio.

Key KPI:

The most important KPI is **current cloud backlog**.

SAP defines CCB as contractually committed cloud revenue expected to be recognized over the next twelve months.

That makes it a relatively high-quality cRPO analogue.

At **€22.9B and +26% cc**, it provides substantial evidence that large enterprise migrations remain intact even as recognized revenue growth moderates.

Guidance headline:

FY26 guidance:

Cloud revenue: **€25.8B-€26.2B cc**, +23%-25%.

Cloud and software revenue: **€36.3B-€36.8B cc**, +12%-13%.

Non-IFRS operating profit: **€11.8B-€12.2B cc**, +13%-17%.

FCF: approximately **€10B**.

Current cloud backlog growth is expected to **slightly decelerate** during 2026 from the prior-year level.

The operating-profit reduction from €11.9B-€12.3B reflects more than €100M of expected dilution from Dremio and Prior Labs.

Capital allocation:

SAP's €10B buyback programme runs through December 2027.

As of June 30, SAP had repurchased approximately **€2.6B** of stock under the programme at an average price of €161.16.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Clean to Mixed.

Adjustment issue:

SAP reports material IFRS/non-IFRS differences, primarily including:

* share-based compensation;
* acquisition-related charges;
* restructuring;
* certain gains/losses and tax effects.

However, **Dremio and Prior Labs are not Q2 non-operating adjustments**.

They closed in July.

Their >€100M impact belongs in the **forward guidance bridge**, not the Q2 accounting-quality register.

Reltio, already consolidated, contributed **less than 1 percentage point** to CCB growth.

PM interpretation:

The Q2 operating state is not being flattered by a material one-off.

The key normalization is simply to use non-IFRS operating metrics consistently.

The small FY guide trim should be viewed as intentional acquisition dilution.

| ----------------- | ---------------------: | ------: | ---------------------: | ------------------------ | ------------------------------------------------------------------ |
