GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The key strategic distinction is between **AI attach** and **AI monetization**.

SAP disclosed that Business AI and Business Data Cloud capabilities appeared in more than 90% of its top 50 Q2 deals.

That demonstrates:

**AI relevance inside enterprise purchasing decisions.**

It does **not** yet demonstrate:

**a separately measurable AI revenue stream or token economics sufficient to drive group growth independently.**

SAP's stronger moat remains the installed ERP/data/process layer.

AI increases the strategic value of migrating those workloads into SAP's cloud because autonomous agents require clean enterprise process data and permissions.

The commercial chain is therefore:

**mission-critical ERP → cloud migration → structured enterprise data → embedded AI functionality → higher suite relevance / potential future consumption monetization.**

Revision direction: **Mix-shift revisions.**

Cloud/backlog estimates remain constructive.

Near-term operating-profit estimates decline approximately €100M due to Dremio/Prior Labs dilution.

Longer-term AI monetization remains upside optionality rather than fully quantified base-case revenue.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

SAP is one of the cleanest counters to the broad **AI destroys SaaS seats** thesis.

The business is not primarily selling discretionary point-solution seats.

It controls core finance, supply-chain, HR and enterprise-process systems.

Those workloads are difficult to replace and increasingly valuable when combined with enterprise AI.

The 26% CCB growth is therefore more important than commentary about AI enthusiasm.

It shows that customers are putting contractual money behind migrations.

However, the print should not be over-interpreted.

Recognized cloud revenue grew 24% cc.

Cloud gross margin declined modestly.

The company did not disclose a standalone Business AI ARR/revenue line.

So the current evidence is:

**cloud durability proven; AI attach proven; standalone AI monetization not yet separately proven.**

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The first Q&A question also matters.

Morgan Stanley's Adam Wood did **not** primarily ask about recognized-cloud-revenue timing.

He challenged the reduced operating-profit guide and asked whether SAP had less room for future margin expansion than investors believed.

That is the correct institutional debate.

The revenue thesis looks strong.

The valuation question is now how much incremental AI/R&D and acquisition spending SAP must absorb to sustain it.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

The prior concern was whether broader enterprise-software disruption and AI-native competitors would begin weakening SAP's migration pipeline.

Q2 did not support that bear case.

CCB remained above recognized cloud growth and reached €22.9B.

The debate therefore moves downstream from demand durability toward **margin conversion and AI economics**.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

SAP rose roughly **10%** in Frankfurt on July 24 and led the European technology sector higher.

That reaction is instructive.

The company technically **lowered** one guidance line.

But the stock strongly rerated because the market isolated the cause:

Dremio/Prior Labs dilution.

Against that, investors received:

* CCB +26% cc;
* cloud +24%;
* Cloud ERP Suite +27%;
* FCF +27%.

The market therefore treated the guide trim as **strategic reinvestment**, not weakening core economics.

This is another example of the season's evidence hierarchy:

**contracted demand > superficial guidance optics.**

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder; software durability; expensive but deliverable.**

SAP is increasingly valued as the enterprise-data/process control plane rather than a legacy ERP vendor.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

Frankfurt shares gained approximately **10%** on July 24.

The ADR also rallied strongly.

The read-across is constructive for enterprise software companies whose products sit directly in mission-critical workflows.

It is less constructive for discretionary seat-based SaaS.

SAP's evidence suggests AI can reinforce the value of systems of record rather than simply displace them.

The relevant peer question is:

**Does AI sit on top of the application's proprietary data/process moat, or does it commoditize the application's user interface?**

SAP currently sits firmly in the first category.

AI / BACKLOG CHECK

Current cloud backlog:

€22.929B.

Growth:

+26% cc.

Cloud revenue:

€6.281B.

Growth:

+24% cc.

Cloud gross margin:

74.6%.

AI attach:

> 90% of top 50 deals included Business AI / Business Data Cloud capabilities according to management commentary.

The next evidence threshold is not more deal anecdotes.

It is either:

* disclosed AI revenue/consumption growth;
* higher suite ACV attributable to AI;
* or improved cloud revenue/margin conversion tied explicitly to Business AI.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Current cloud backlog / Why it matters: Falling below ~20% cc would weaken the core migration-duration thesis.

2. CCB-to-cloud-revenue conversion / Why it matters: Sustained divergence could indicate implementation delays or growing duration.

3. Cloud gross margin / Why it matters: AI compute and platform investment must eventually be monetized rather than permanently dilute margin.

4. Business AI monetization / Why it matters: High attach rates need to translate into measurable economics.

5. Dremio/Prior Labs integration / Why it matters: The acquisitions must justify the >€100M FY dilution through better data/agent capabilities.

BOTTOM LINE

SAP delivered a **high-quality cloud backlog print with a strategically benign guidance trim**.

Current cloud backlog reached **€22.9B and +26% cc**.

Cloud revenue grew **24% cc**.

Cloud ERP Suite grew **27% cc**.

FCF increased **27%**.

The operating-profit guide fell only because of newly closed AI/data acquisitions.

The strongest defensible state transition is therefore:

**Improvement — cloud durability and AI attach strengthened.**

I would stop short of **“agentic monetization validated.”**

SAP has shown that AI is increasingly embedded in enterprise deals.

It has not yet shown a separately measurable AI revenue engine.

The next rerating stage requires exactly that conversion.

EXEC SUMMARY

ASM International reported record Q2 revenue of **€1.003B**, up 20% reported and 24% at constant currencies, above the midpoint of its €980M ±5% guidance. Leading-edge logic/foundry remained the main driver, with accelerating 2nm demand, while HBM-related DRAM also strengthened.

Gross margin was **51.9%**, adjusted operating profit **€330.5M**, adjusted operating margin **33.0%**, and adjusted net earnings **€292.9M**, up 69% YoY. FCF reached a record **€355M** as working capital normalized after the Q1 build.

Management guided Q3 revenue to **€1.10B ±5% at constant currency**, expects H2 revenue to exceed H1 by more than 20%, and now expects 2027 revenue to exceed the previous €3.7B-€4.6B target range. But expectations were even higher: analysts were looking for roughly €4.9B in 2027, while FY26 gross margin around 51% was also below the ~51.5% bar. That mismatch drove the negative initial reaction.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 revenue reached **€1,003.1M**.

Q1 revenue was €862.5M.

Q2 2025 revenue was €835.6M.

Reported YoY growth was 20%.

Constant-currency growth was 24%.

Management cited:

* strong leading-edge logic/foundry;
* robust HBM DRAM;
* continued solid mature logic/foundry sales in China.

AI-related applications remained a major driver of customer investment.

Consensus:

The company beat the midpoint of its own Q2 guidance.

Reliable comprehensive Q2 Street consensus was not confirmed.

The key forward disappointment was more important:

the new 2027 statement—**above €4.6B**—was below more aggressive analyst expectations around €4.9B.

EPS / Earnings:

Reported net earnings reached **€285.4M**.

Adjusted net earnings reached **€292.9M**.

Reported diluted EPS was approximately **€5.80**.

Adjusted net earnings increased €120M YoY.

Profitability / Margins:

Gross profit reached **€521.1M**.

Gross margin was **51.9%**, essentially flat YoY and below Q1's unusually strong 53.3%.

Operating profit was €322.8M.

Adjusted operating profit was **€330.5M**.

Adjusted operating margin reached **33.0%**, versus 31.5% one year earlier.

The company maintained strong margins while continuing high R&D investment.

Key KPI:

The central structural KPI is **process-step intensity per advanced wafer**, not total WFE alone.

GAA architectures require more deposition complexity.

ASM's opportunity increases as customers move from:

FinFET 3nm
→ GAA 2nm
→ GAA 1.4nm.

Its own Investor Day framework estimated an incremental **$450M-$500M serviceable market per 100k wafer starts/month** moving from 2nm to 1.4nm, driven by added ALD/Epi layers and process complexity.

Guidance headline:

Q3 revenue:

**€1.10B ±5% at constant currency.**

H2 revenue:

**more than 20% above H1 at constant currency.**

Drivers:

* leading-edge logic/foundry;
* initial 1.4nm contribution;
* memory/HBM;
* some recovery in power/analog/wafer.

Mature logic/foundry is expected to decline from its H1 level.

2027:

Revenue expected to exceed the previous **€4.6B** upper target.

Further guidance will come during 2027.

Capital allocation:

Q2 FCF reached a record **€355M**.

The improvement came from strong profitability and normalization of working capital after Q1 inventory/receivables buildup.

ASM continues substantial R&D investment to capture future advanced-node transitions.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Clean to Mixed.

Adjustment issue:

Adjusted operating profit was €330.5M versus reported €322.8M.

Adjusted net earnings were €292.9M versus reported €285.4M.

The gaps are small.

The more material earnings-comparison issue is **currency translation below the operating line**.

Q2 2026 adjusted net earnings included approximately **€22M of currency-translation gains**, versus approximately **€60M of losses** in Q2 2025.

That favorable €82M swing materially amplified the +69% YoY adjusted net-income growth.

PM interpretation:

Operating-profit and margin growth are the cleaner measures.

The revenue and operating-margin improvement is genuine.

The 69% adjusted net-income growth overstates the pure operating acceleration because of the large FX comparison swing.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The most important correction to the preliminary tape is terminology.

ASM does **not** have a “2nm GAA monopoly.”

The defensible thesis is:

**GAA materially increases ALD/Epi content, and ASM is gaining share in several critical deposition steps.**

At 2nm, leading-edge demand is already accelerating.

At 1.4nm, management expects first meaningful H2 contribution and explicitly disclosed additional share gains in ALD and Epi.

The company also confirmed recent **molybdenum ALD wins** at 1.4nm.

That is a high-value process win because molybdenum is one of the materials being evaluated for increasingly difficult interconnect/contact structures at advanced nodes.

But a process win is not a monopoly.

Revision direction: **Upward revisions / mix-shift revisions.**

H2 revenue estimates move higher.

2027 estimates move above ASM's previous range.

Gross-margin assumptions move slightly lower versus the most aggressive expectations.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

ASMI is an important complement to ASML and Applied Materials.

ASML monetizes lithography complexity.

Applied Materials monetizes a broad set of deposition, etch and process-control steps.

ASM is unusually concentrated in **atomic-scale deposition and epitaxy**, where structural layer intensity rises as transistor architecture becomes more three-dimensional and materially complex.

That means ASMI can outperform WFE even if aggregate fab spending is less explosive.

The mechanism is:

**more advanced node → more complex structure → more deposition steps → higher ALD/Epi tool intensity per wafer-start.**

The Q2 print confirms that mechanism in both logic and memory.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The strongest positive signal is **H2 >20% sequential growth versus H1**.

The second is the 1.4nm timing.

Management says first meaningful contribution begins in H2 2026 rather than remaining a distant 2027/28 concept.

The third is HBM.

Memory is becoming a second growth leg rather than leaving ASMI entirely dependent on foundry logic.

The first Q&A question from JPMorgan's Sandeep Deshpande focused on **2027 revenue phasing**, not customer mix between TSMC/Samsung/Intel.

That reinforces where institutional attention actually sat:

the market wanted to know how much above €4.6B ASM could deliver.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q1 had already demonstrated strong advanced-node demand and pushed the stock sharply higher.

Q2 confirmed the operating acceleration.

But that created an elevated valuation bar.

The debate has therefore moved from:

**"Will GAA create incremental ALD intensity?"**

to:

**"How much above the already-raised 2027 expectations can that intensity drive revenue?"**

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

The original +5.4% reaction is incorrect for the direct post-print window.

ASM released after the July 28 close.

On July 29, shares fell **4.76% to €720**, after trading as low as €683.60 and down roughly 8.4% intraday.

Two issues drove the disappointment:

1. FY26 gross margin around 51% versus approximately 51.5% expected.
2. 2027 revenue simply above €4.6B versus Street estimates closer to €4.9B.

The selloff was also amplified by contemporaneous concerns about China's progress in domestic DUV lithography equipment, which hit Dutch semiconductor-equipment names broadly.

Then on July 30, ASMI rebounded **9.53%**.

So the correct classification is:

**release-specific negative + sector amplification → rapid fundamental rebound.**

This is not evidence that the print was weak.

It is evidence that expectations were extreme.

Stock identity:

**Secular growth at a cyclical multiple; expensive but deliverable; crowded long; setup-risk stock.**

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

July 28 close: €756.00.

July 29 close: **€720.00, -4.76%**.

July 30 close: **€788.60, +9.53%**.

The two-session reversal shows that the market initially punished guidance relative to expectations, then re-engaged with the structural AI/WFE thesis.

Read-across:

**ASML:** positive advanced-node intensity, but ASMI's demand is less tied to lithography booking lumpiness.

**AMAT:** confirms strong deposition/material engineering spend.

**TSMC / Samsung / Intel:** leading-edge transition schedules remain the critical end-demand driver.

**Memory:** HBM-driven DRAM capex is becoming increasingly important.

ADVANCED WFE CHECK

2nm:

Demand accelerating.

1.4nm:

First meaningful revenue contribution expected H2 2026.

Mo ALD:

Recent wins confirmed.

HBM DRAM:

Strong Q2 contribution, with another DRAM customer selecting ASM's Epi solution.

2027:

Revenue >€4.6B.

This is strong structural evidence.

But the correct market-instrument hierarchy is:

**customer equipment plans / orders → node qualification → tool shipment → revenue → gross margin.**

ASMI does not disclose a simple dollar backlog instrument comparable to MSFT RPO or CAT backlog in the current release.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. H2 revenue growth / Why it matters: Failure to exceed H1 by >20% cc would directly break the current acceleration thesis.

2. 1.4nm contribution / Why it matters: Early H2 revenue is the strongest proof that technology wins are moving into commercial shipments.

3. Gross margin / Why it matters: ~51% is excellent, but further compression would indicate mix/cost pressure despite high demand.

4. HBM / DRAM ALD and Epi / Why it matters: Memory diversification increases the duration and breadth of the growth cycle.

5. 2027 customer plans / Why it matters: The market already expects materially more than €4.6B; simply meeting the upgraded company target may not be enough.

BOTTOM LINE

ASM International delivered a **decisive operating improvement but failed an exceptionally high valuation bar**.

Q2 revenue exceeded €1B for the first time.

Revenue grew 24% at constant currency.

Adjusted operating margin remained 33%.

H2 revenue is expected to exceed H1 by more than 20%.

2027 revenue is now expected above the previous €4.6B upper target.

The GAA thesis is clearly strengthening.

But the stock initially fell because investors already expected something closer to €4.9B and slightly better margins.

The correct state is therefore:

**Improvement — structural ALD/Epi intensity accelerating, but already heavily priced.**

The next rerating requires not just 2nm/GAA adoption.

It requires **2027 revenue visibility meaningfully above today's already aggressive Street numbers while maintaining ~51%+ gross margins.**

EXEC SUMMARY

UBS reported Q2 net profit of $2.8B, reported PBT of $3.6B and underlying PBT of $3.9B. Underlying PBT increased 45% YoY and underlying RoCET1 reached 16.4%. Group invested assets reached a record $7.3T. The operating result was broad rather than dependent on a single division.

Global Wealth Management generated $36B of net new assets and $13B of net new fee-generating assets. GWM PBT rose 38% to $2.0B, transaction-based income increased 23%, and NII rose 12% YoY to $1.8B. Management now expects full-year GWM NII to increase approximately 10% versus 2025, supported by loan growth, higher USD rates and better deposit mix.

The second major pillar was integration. UBS has realized $12.6B of cumulative gross cost savings versus a roughly $13.5B year-end goal, with more than 90% of legacy Credit Suisse applications no longer in use. A new $3B buyback was announced, but its pace remains explicitly conditional on clarity around Swiss rules governing the capitalization of foreign subsidiaries.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

UBS reported Q2 revenues of approximately $13.3B, up 16% YoY on the basis used in management's earnings-call remarks.

Growth was broad across the core franchises.

Global Wealth Management revenues increased 14%.

Investment Bank revenues increased 31%.

Personal & Corporate Banking revenues increased 3%.

Asset Management revenues declined 2% reported, but increased 5% excluding business-exit effects.

Consensus:

Net profit of $2.8B exceeded the approximately $2.39B company-conducted analyst consensus cited by Reuters.

Third-party services reported adjusted EPS of $0.87 versus approximately $0.90 consensus.

Those two comparisons use different analytical bases and should not be collapsed into a single “beat” or “miss.”

EPS / Earnings:

Reported net profit was $2.8B.

Reported PBT was $3.6B.

Underlying PBT was $3.9B.

Underlying RoCET1 was 16.4%.

H1 underlying RoCET1 reached 16.7%.

UBS therefore entered H2 already above its approximately 15% 2026 exit-rate return target.

Profitability / Margins:

Underlying cost/income ratio was 70%.

The core businesses generated 47% YoY underlying PBT growth on a combined basis.

UBS produced approximately eight percentage points of positive operating leverage at group level.

The quality of that leverage is increasingly important because the large Credit Suisse restructuring benefits are nearing completion.

Key KPI:

The highest-value KPI is not total AUM alone.

It is the combination of:

$36B net new assets;
$13B net new fee-generating assets;
$7B net new GWM loans;
record mandate penetration;
My Way invested assets above $40B, +75% YoY.

That indicates UBS is deepening monetization per client rather than relying only on asset-market appreciation.

Guidance headline:

UBS expects:

- substantially completed Credit Suisse integration by year-end;
- cumulative gross savings of approximately $13.5B by YE26;
- underlying cost/income ratio below 70% at the 2026 exit rate;
- the ability to outperform the approximately 15% underlying RoCET1 exit-rate target;
- FY26 GWM NII growth of approximately 10% versus 2025.

Management expects approximately $750M of remaining integration-related expenses in H2, roughly evenly split between Q3 and Q4.

Capital allocation:

UBS announced a new $3B share-repurchase programme to be completed by the end of Q2 2027 at the latest.

At least $1B is intended for the following three months.

The full programme was already accrued against CET1 capital in Q2.

That accrual reduced the CET1 ratio by approximately 60bp.

This is a capital-accounting effect.

It did not reduce Q2 net income or EPS.

Future buyback speed remains conditional on:

financial performance;
maintaining CET1 around 14%;
and clarity from Swiss Parliament on foreign-subsidiary capitalization.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed but transparently reconciled.

Adjustment issue:

Reported PBT of $3.6B included:

- $352M of revenue adjustments;
- $645M of integration expenses.

Underlying PBT was $3.9B.

These remain meaningful differences, but they are directly tied to the final stages of the Credit Suisse integration rather than recurring operating weakness.

The integration expense run-rate should fall significantly once the programme is completed.

PM interpretation:

Underlying measures remain the cleaner operating lens for 2026.

But the important transition is that this normalization is close to ending.

Once Credit Suisse integration costs disappear, UBS loses a large pool of obvious adjustment add-backs and must increasingly generate EPS growth from:

wealth inflows;
higher fee penetration;
NII;
IB revenue;
and conventional cost discipline.

The $3B buyback accrual belongs in the capital-quality section, not the earnings-quality bridge.

SEGMENT TABLE

|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

UBS is not best treated as a simple European NII trade.

Its earnings stack is:

**global wealth fees**
+
**USD-sensitive GWM NII**
+
**capital-markets activity**
+
**Swiss banking**
+
**Credit Suisse cost normalization.**

That makes monetary-policy transmission more complex than for a Eurozone retail bank.

Lower CHF and EUR rates pressure deposit economics.

But UBS explicitly says higher USD rates, loan growth and improved deposit mix more than offset those effects in its updated GWM NII guide.

The policy lens should therefore be:

**currency mix + client balance-sheet mix**, not simply SNB cuts = UBS negative.

Revision direction: Upward revisions.

GWM NII assumptions should rise.

IB revenue estimates remain constructive.

Cost estimates improve as integration savings arrive earlier.

Capital-return estimates remain conditional on Swiss regulation.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

The quarter validates the Credit Suisse transaction operationally.

Three years after the acquisition, UBS has:

migrated all clients;
taken more than 90% of legacy applications out of use;
captured $12.6B of gross cost savings;
reduced Non-core and Legacy costs dramatically;
and returned group profitability toward pre-acquisition levels.

The key analytical shift is therefore:

**the integration story is approaching completion.**

That is positive because execution risk falls.

It is also important for valuation because the easy synergy bridge is almost exhausted.

Future earnings growth must increasingly be organic.

The encouraging part of Q2 is that organic engines appear strong enough:

GWM flows remain high;
fee-generating asset growth is healthy;
lending is accelerating;
Investment Bank revenue is growing without material balance-sheet expansion.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The additional tape corrects the first-Q&A record.

The first analyst was **Jeremy Sigee of BNP Paribas**.

His two questions went directly to the important strategic issues:

1. how UBS produced large Investment Bank revenue growth without expanding the balance sheet;
2. whether U.S. Wealth advisor departures threaten stabilization of the Americas franchise.

Management said IB capital was actively reallocated toward higher-return Equities activity, while previously announced U.S. adviser exits will continue affecting flows for several quarters but should normalize during 2026.

That is more informative than the later Jefferies NII question.

State transition: Continuation

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

Q1 had already delivered $37B of GWM net new assets, 17% underlying RoCET1 and very strong Investment Bank revenues.

Q2 broadly repeated that operating strength.

What changed incrementally is that integration completion is now close enough for management to quantify the remaining H2 expense tail and commit to another $3B buyback.

The debate therefore moves from Credit Suisse execution risk toward normalized earnings power and regulatory capital.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

The stock initially rose around 3.5%.

It closed only 0.9% higher.

This does not look like rejection of the earnings quality.

Net profit beat.

Wealth flows were strong.

IB returns were exceptional.

Cost savings were ahead of schedule.

The fade is better explained by a combination of:

strong pre-print performance;
limited incremental surprise after a very strong Q1;
and the unresolved Swiss capital framework governing future distributions.

The third-party $0.87 versus $0.90 EPS miss may have contributed to headline noise, but it should not be treated as a fundamental driver and certainly not as an effect of the buyback accrual.

The actual capital-return debate is larger:

**How much excess capital can UBS distribute once Switzerland finalizes the post-Credit-Suisse regulatory regime?**

That question has more valuation impact than a $0.03 quarterly EPS variance.

Stock identity:

Quality compounder; capital-markets franchise; wealth compounder; regulatory setup-risk stock.

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

July 28 close: CHF42.44.

July 29 close: **CHF42.82, +0.90%**.

Intraday high: approximately **CHF43.99**, roughly +3.5%.

July 30 close: **CHF42.62, -0.47%**.

The reaction should therefore be classified:

**Positive initial fundamental response → partial setup/regulatory fade.**

The read-across for European banking is differentiated.

UBS does not require rising Eurozone NII to generate earnings growth.

Its strongest Q2 contributors were:

wealth fee income;
USD-sensitive NII;
capital markets;
integration savings.

That makes UBS closer to a hybrid of a global wealth manager and U.S.-style capital-markets bank than a conventional European commercial bank.

WEALTH / RATES CHECK

GWM NII:

$1.8B.

YoY:

+12%.

Full-year guidance:

approximately +10% versus 2025.

Drivers:

higher loan volumes;
higher USD rates;
better deposit mix.

Offset:

margin compression in lower-rate currencies.

This is the correct policy-divergence instrument.

The quarter therefore argues against treating SNB easing as automatically bearish for UBS earnings.

The franchise's global currency and lending mix creates an important offset.

SWISS CAPITAL CHECK

CET1 ratio:

14.4%.

New buyback:

$3B.

Immediate intended execution:

at least $1B over three months.

CET1 effect already accrued:

approximately -60bp.

The regulatory overhang remains the most important non-operating variable for the equity.

If Switzerland requires materially higher capital backing for foreign subsidiaries, future buybacks could be slower or smaller even if operating earnings remain strong.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Swiss capital rules / Why it matters: This is now the principal constraint on the capital-return multiple.

2. GWM net new fee-generating assets / Why it matters: These are more valuable than headline AUM because they drive recurring fee revenue.

3. Americas adviser attrition / Why it matters: Previously announced departures will continue affecting U.S. flows for several quarters.

4. GWM NII / Why it matters: Meeting the ~10% FY growth guide would confirm that USD rates and loan growth can offset lower CHF/EUR margins.

5. Investment Bank capital efficiency / Why it matters: Sustaining >20% returns without expanding the balance sheet materially upgrades the earnings mix.

BOTTOM LINE

UBS delivered another **strong operating quarter**, but the better state label is **Continuation**, not a new fundamental acceleration.

Net profit was $2.8B.

Underlying PBT increased 45%.

GWM gathered another $36B of assets.

Investment Bank revenues increased 31% and generated more than $1B of PBT.

Credit Suisse integration is approaching completion.

The new $3B buyback demonstrates confidence in capital generation.

The key change in the investment case is therefore where the risk now sits.

It is moving away from:

**Can UBS integrate Credit Suisse?**

toward:

**What is normalized organic earnings growth once synergies are exhausted, and how much of that capital can shareholders actually receive under the final Swiss rules?**

Q2 answers the first question constructively.

The second remains the main valuation constraint.

EXEC SUMMARY

HSBC reported Q2 revenue of **$19.12B**, up 16% YoY, and reported profit before tax of **$10.1B**, up 60%. But those headline growth rates contain a large comparison benefit from notable items. On HSBC's cleaner constant-currency basis excluding notable items, Q2 revenue was **$19.04B, +8%**, while management described underlying PBT of approximately **$10.3B, +13%**.

The core operating indicators improved. Banking NII increased to **$11.64B in Q2**, up 9% YoY and 3% QoQ. Customer lending increased **$20B sequentially**, deposits increased **$46B**, and Wealth fee and other income increased **22% YoY** to $2.77B. Management raised FY26 banking-NII guidance to **at least $46B** while retaining its target of RoTE of at least 17% for 2026-28.

Capital returns restarted with a **$1B buyback** and a second interim dividend of **$0.10/share**. The negative offset is credit: Q2 ECL charges were $1.1B, including $0.2B related to Hong Kong commercial real estate, while FY26 ECL guidance remains around **45bp**, above HSBC's medium-term 30-40bp planning range.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Reported Q2 revenue was **$19.118B**, versus $16.473B a year earlier.

That is +16% reported.

However, Q2 2025 included **-$1.184B of notable revenue items**, while Q2 2026 contained +$75M.

Excluding notable items, revenue was:

Q2 2026: **$19.043B**.

Q2 2025: **$17.657B**.

Underlying growth was therefore approximately **8%**, not 16%.

Management described constant-currency revenue growth excluding notable items at approximately 7%-8% depending the presentation base.

The underlying contributors were:

banking NII;
Wealth;
Wholesale Transaction Banking;
Debt and Equity Markets.

Consensus:

H1 reported PBT of $19.5B exceeded approximately **$18.9B consensus** cited by Reuters.

Q2 revenue also exceeded the roughly $18.5B figure in the supplied scout tape.

The operating beat is valid, but comparison bases need to remain consistent.

EPS / Earnings:

Reported Q2 PBT:

**$10.1B.**

Reported Q2 profit after tax:

**$7.9B.**

Management's constant-currency/ex-notables presentation described Q2 PBT at approximately **$10.3B, +13%**.

These are not contradictory.

They are different bases.

The supplied **$2.30 EPS** figure should not be archived as Q2 ordinary-share EPS.

HSBC's SEC filing reports basic Q2 EPS on the ordinary-share basis at approximately **$0.45**.

Profitability / Margins:

H1 annualized reported RoTE was **18.2%**.

Excluding notable items:

**19.1%**.

Q2 management presentation:

approximately **19.5%**.

All four core businesses delivered annualized returns above HSBC's >=17% group target, according to management.

Key KPI:

The decisive banking KPI is the combination:

**banking NII + balance-sheet growth + fee growth.**

Q2 banking NII:

$11.643B.

QoQ:

+3%.

YoY:

+9%.

Customer loans:

+$20B QoQ.

Deposits:

+$46B QoQ.

Wealth fees:

+22% YoY.

This is materially better evidence than simply saying "Asia shields NII."

The actual mechanism is:

**favourable rate environment + deposit base + hedge income + loan growth + high-fee Asian wealth activity.**

Guidance headline:

FY26 banking NII:

**at least $46B.**

RoTE:

**>=17% in 2026, 2027 and 2028**, excluding notable items.

positive growth through 2028, reaching approximately 5% growth in 2028 on a constant-currency/ex-notables basis.

target 50%.

approximately +1% in 2026 on target basis.

medium-term **14%-14.5%**.

approximately **45bp** for 2026.

Medium-term ECL planning range:

30%-40bp.

Capital allocation:

HSBC resumed share repurchases with a **$1B buyback**.

The programme began August 6 and is due to finish no later than the Q3 results announcement.

The Board also approved a **$0.10 second interim dividend**.

The buyback was smaller than some investors expected, which likely contributed to the subdued print-day response. Reuters noted Citi analysts specifically questioned its size.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed.

Adjustment issue:

The strongest correction to the scout tape is here.

**ECL is not a non-operating §13 item.**

Expected credit losses are a core banking operating expense and should be assessed as part of fundamental earnings quality.

Q2 ECL was **$1.1B**, broadly stable YoY.

It included approximately **$0.2B of Hong Kong CRE stage-3 charges**, versus approximately $0.4B in Q2 2025.

The more important accounting distortion is the notable-item comparison.

Q2 2025 contained:

**$2.1B of BoCom dilution and impairment losses**.

Q2 2026 contained:

approximately **$0.2B of organisational-simplification restructuring charges**.

At H1 level, HSBC says the reported PBT increase benefited from a **$2.2B favourable YoY notable-item effect**.

PM interpretation:

Reported PBT +60% Q2 and +23% H1 significantly overstate the underlying operating acceleration.

The cleaner H1 comparison is:

revenue ex-notables +6% cc;
PBT ex-notables +6% cc.

That is still a good operating result.

It is simply much less explosive than the headline profit growth.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The correct policy-divergence framework is more nuanced than:

**ECB/BoE cuts → Asia shields HSBC.**

HSBC's banking-NII outcome depends on several components:

policy rates across multiple currencies;
structural hedges;
deposit mix;
loan volumes;
Hong Kong and UK rate transmission.

Management's first Q&A is particularly useful.

**Guy Stebbings of BNP Paribas** asked why H2 banking NII should not grow more strongly given the Q2 run rate, hedge support and improving volumes.

That is the institutional debate.

The market is no longer primarily worried about imminent NII collapse.

It is asking how much additional upside the current rate/volume environment can generate.

Revision direction: **Upward revisions.**

Banking-NII estimates should rise toward at least $46B.

Wealth and lending assumptions improve.

Credit-cost assumptions remain elevated around 45bp.

Capital-return estimates improve with the buyback restart, but $1B was below some expectations.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

HSBC's fundamental engine is becoming more balanced.

The old shorthand was:

**Asia + NII.**

The current earnings mix is:

**banking NII**
+
**Asia Wealth**
+
**Wholesale Transaction Banking**
+
**loan growth**
+
**capital-markets activity.**

That matters because the bank is less dependent on a single rate path than a domestic retail bank.

The strongest secular component is wealth.

H1 Wealth fee and other income reached **$5.5B**, versus $4.6B a year earlier.

In International Wealth and Premier Banking alone, Wealth fees grew **18%**, with strength across Hong Kong, mainland China, Singapore and Taiwan.

But the direct link:

**HSBC Asia revenue → LVMH/Richemont demand**

is too strong.

HSBC Wealth activity tells us that Asian financial wealth creation and cross-border activity are healthy.

It does not establish luxury consumption causality.

The appropriate read-across is:

**supportive UHNW / affluent Asia financial backdrop**, not a direct luxury-sales transmission chain.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The first Q&A was from **Guy Stebbings, BNP Paribas**.

His questions focused on:

1. whether organic growth opportunities now deserve capital ahead of buybacks;
2. why H2 banking NII guidance was not higher given Q2's run rate, hedge support and stronger balance volumes.

That captures the real institutional tension.

HSBC has moved from a defensive restructuring story toward a capital-allocation question:

**reinvest in high-return Asia/cross-border growth or distribute excess capital?**

The $1B buyback suggests management currently sees substantial reinvestment opportunities.

State transition: Improvement

CONTEXT FROM PRIOR QUARTER / PRIOR DEBATE

The main pre-print debate was whether easing policy rates would begin eroding HSBC's historically strong NII.

Q2 pushed against that thesis.

Banking NII increased both YoY and sequentially.

Loans and deposits expanded.

Management raised FY guidance.

At the same time, credit costs remain above medium-term levels and the Hang Seng privatization has reduced surplus CET1.

The debate therefore moves from **NII downside** toward **growth-versus-distribution capital allocation**.

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

The earnings-day reaction was modestly negative.

HSBA closed August 4 at **1,584.6p, -0.8%**.

That reaction makes sense despite the operating beat.

The setup was already strong.

The $1B buyback was less than some expected.

And the headline profit growth contained material notable-item assistance.

The much larger move occurred the following day:

August 5 close:

**1,510.6p, -4.67%.**

That move should **not** be attributed to earnings.

Caixin reported that mainland Chinese authorities had begun levying a 20% personal-income tax on returns from some offshore Hong Kong insurance products.

HSBC fell as much as approximately 6% alongside Prudential and other Hong Kong financial names.

This creates an important new post-print risk for the HSBC thesis:

**cross-border Chinese wealth regulation.**

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder; yield / income stock; rates-sensitive bank; Asia wealth compounder.**

MARKET REACTION / PEER READ-ACROSS

Release-specific reaction:

**Aug 4: -0.8%.**

External policy shock:

**Aug 5: -4.7%.**

These must remain separate in the archive.

The August 5 event is actually more informative for HSBC's strategic risk than the earnings-day move.

HSBC has intentionally concentrated more of its growth strategy around:

Hong Kong;
Asian wealth;
cross-border flows.

That creates stronger structural growth.

It also creates greater sensitivity to Beijing policy governing offshore financial products and capital movement.

RATES / CREDIT CHECK

Banking NII Q2:

$11.64B.

Sequential:

+3%.

FY guide:

> = $46B.

Conclusion:

NII resilience is improving.

ECL Q2:

$1.1B.

FY ECL ratio guide:

~45bp.

Medium-term:

30-40bp.

Conclusion:

Credit remains **above normalized levels**, not a benign footnote.

Hong Kong CRE stage-3 charges:

$0.2B Q2.

Prior year:

$0.4B.

Conclusion:

Direction improving, but not fully resolved.

This is the correct rates-versus-credit scoreboard.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Banking NII / Why it matters: >=$46B is now the central earnings guide and tests whether rate/volume support outweighs eventual easing.

2. Wealth fees / Why it matters: Double-digit growth is the structural non-NII engine and reduces rate dependence.

3. China offshore-wealth regulation / Why it matters: The August 5 tax shock directly targets one of HSBC's highest-growth cross-border franchises.

4. Hong Kong CRE ECL / Why it matters: Credit costs remain above the medium-term planning range despite improved Q2 charges.

5. Capital allocation / Why it matters: A $1B buyback versus strong organic growth opportunities signals management may increasingly favor reinvestment over maximum distributions.

BOTTOM LINE

HSBC delivered a **clear underlying improvement**, but the reported headline materially overstates the magnitude.

Reported Q2 PBT rose **60% to $10.1B**.

But the cleaner operating view was approximately **$10.3B of PBT, +13%**, because last year's quarter contained a large BoCom impairment.

Underlying revenue grew approximately **8%**.

Banking NII rose **9%**.

Wealth fees rose **22%**.

Loans increased $20B sequentially.

Deposits increased $46B.

Management raised banking-NII guidance to **at least $46B**.

That is a high-quality operating progression.

But credit costs remain elevated at a ~45bp FY run-rate assumption and the August 5 China offshore-insurance tax shock demonstrates the regulatory sensitivity created by HSBC's increasing Hong Kong/Asia concentration.

The correct state transition is therefore:

**Improvement — stronger NII, wealth and balance-sheet growth, but with elevated credit costs and increasing China-policy sensitivity.**

The key forward question is no longer simply whether HSBC can withstand falling Western rates.

It is:

**Can HSBC compound its Asia/cross-border franchise while maintaining credit quality and avoiding regulatory erosion of the Hong Kong wealth proposition?**

THEME_BUCKETS: Credit / Banks, Rates Sensitivity, Capital Markets, Turnaround / Self-Help
THEMES: net interest income, Spain banking, TSB integration, Webster acquisition, fee growth, customer growth, efficiency, Latin America credit, capital returns, ONE Transformation
SEGMENT_TABLE_STATUS: Partial
SOURCE_ROLE: Brief note

EXEC SUMMARY

Santander reported **€3.768B of Q2 underlying profit, +17% YoY**, slightly ahead of approximately €3.75B consensus. Reported attributable profit was €3.52B, +3%, after **€250M of TSB restructuring costs**. H1 underlying profit reached a record **€7.328B, +15%**.

The revenue mix was strong. Q2 NII increased **10% YoY to €11.69B**, ahead of approximately €11.47B consensus, while fees increased 12%. At H1 level NII rose 7% and fees 9%. Loans grew 9% and customer funds 11% in constant euros. The efficiency ratio improved to **42.8%**, 2.9pp better YoY.

CET1 finished at **14.0%**, despite the TSB acquisition, versus Santander's 12.8%-13.0% year-end target. The bank reaffirmed 2026 guidance for mid-single-digit revenue growth, lower costs in constant euros and profit above €14.1B. A further approximately **€1.8B H1 buyback** has already received ECB approval.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

H1 revenue reached **€30.847B**, up 6%.

Q2 revenue grew approximately **9% YoY**, while costs rose only about 2%.

The mix improved through both major recurring lines:

NII +10% Q2.

Fees +12% Q2.

At H1:

NII +7%.

Fees +9%.

Reuters explicitly noted Q2 NII of €11.69B exceeded €11.47B consensus.

EPS / Earnings:

Underlying Q2 profit:

**€3.768B, +17%.**

Reported Q2 attributable profit:

**€3.52B, +3%.**

H1 underlying profit:

**€7.328B, +15%.**

H1 reported attributable profit was higher because Q1 included the gain on the disposal of Santander Bank Polska.

Profitability / Margins:

Underlying RoTE reached **15.6%**.

Adjusted for excess capital, Santander describes returns around **17%**.

Efficiency improved to **42.8%**.

Costs excluding TSB fell 2% in constant euros at H1.

This is important: current profit growth is not simply a rates tailwind.

The cost base is becoming more efficient simultaneously.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed but transparent.

Adjustment issue:

Q2 reported profit included **€250M of restructuring costs related to TSB**.

Underlying profit excludes those charges.

At H1 level, statutory earnings are additionally distorted upward by the **€1.895B net capital gain from the disposal of Santander Bank Polska** completed in Q1.

Credit provisions are **not** one-time accounting adjustments.

Loan-loss provisions increased 9% H1 and approximately 13% Q2, with Argentina the main group-level driver and Brazil also receiving investor attention.

Q2 also contained an additional **€39M UK motor-finance impairment**.

PM interpretation:

Underlying Q2 profit is the cleaner measure for operating momentum.

But cost of risk must remain inside the operating thesis rather than being stripped as an exceptional item.

The real quality equation is:

**revenue growth + efficiency gains versus credit normalization.**

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

Santander reaffirmed its 2026 targets, excluding M&A:

Revenue:

**mid-single-digit growth.**

Costs:

**lower in constant euros.**

Profit:

**above €14.1B**, the 2025 level.

CET1:

**12.8%-13.0%.**

The 14.0% H1 CET1 ratio provides significant room versus the target even after TSB.

The ECB has already approved an additional approximately **€1.8B buyback against H1 2026 earnings**.

Together with buybacks against 2025 earnings and the capital released through the Polish disposal, Santander expects to have delivered approximately **€9B** toward its commitment of at least €10B of buybacks across 2025-26.

Revision direction: Upward revisions.

Q2 NII and underlying profit were ahead of consensus.

H1 execution creates upside risk to the >€14.1B FY profit objective.

Credit provisions remain the main downward revision risk.

BUSINESS / STRATEGIC READ

The important development is that Santander is becoming less dependent on pure balance-sheet NII.

Its current earnings stack is:

**NII growth**
+
**fee growth**
+
**cost reduction**
+
**customer acquisition**
+
**global-business scaling.**

Santander added approximately **12M customers YoY**, reaching 182M including about 4M from TSB.

Loans rose 9%.

Customer funds rose 11%.

Fee growth is already outpacing NII.

That is a better earnings-quality mix than a pure rate-spread bank.

TSB adds another developed-market earnings leg.

It contributed two months in Q2 and Santander expects at least **£400M of cost synergies**.

Webster should further increase U.S. weight after completion.

The benefit is geographic diversification.

The cost is greater integration complexity.

RATES / NII CHECK

Santander should not be modeled as:

**ECB easing = NII contraction.**

Q2 directly falsified that simplification.

NII:

+10% YoY.

Spain remained particularly strong.

The mechanisms include:

loan growth;
deposit repricing;
stable margins;
ALCO positioning;
geographic diversification.

The approximately €60B ALCO bond portfolio with a roughly 3.3% yield and long duration also reduces near-term sensitivity to policy-rate changes, according to the supplied earnings-call tape.

The better European-banks framework is therefore:

**policy rate × deposit beta × hedge book × loan volume × asset mix.**

Santander currently scores well across that combination.

CREDIT CHECK

Group cost of risk:

approximately **115bp**.

This is slightly worse than the earlier 100-110bp expectation.

The deterioration is largely concentrated geographically.

Argentina:

primary increase in H1 provisions.

Brazil:

macro environment remains a drag despite higher profit.

UK:

motor-finance provisions remain a specific issue.

But the group NPL ratio improved to **2.93%**, down 7bp sequentially, and management still describes overall credit quality as solid.

The correct interpretation is therefore:

**credit normalization, not a broad deterioration cycle.**

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

Santander gives a useful contrast to HSBC and UBS.

UBS:

wealth + capital markets + cross-currency NII.

HSBC:

Asia wealth + banking NII + transaction banking.

Santander:

**Eurozone/Spain NII + fees + cost transformation + geographic diversification.**

The clean Q2 result demonstrates that European bank earnings do not need rising ECB policy rates to remain strong.

Balance-sheet growth and deposit/hedge management can offset lower benchmark rates.

At the same time, Santander's Latin American operations create a different risk:

credit and FX volatility can dilute otherwise excellent European operating performance.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

Reuters summarized the quarter cleanly:

underlying profit slightly beat;

NII beat;

Spain, UK and U.S. were strong;

Brazil was relatively weaker.

Jefferies described NII and costs as strong while highlighting regional divergence.

The main institutional question is therefore no longer whether Santander can protect revenues through lower European rates.

It is whether the bank can combine:

European NII resilience;
continued fee growth;
TSB/Webster integration;

without allowing Latin American credit costs to absorb the incremental earnings.

State transition: Improvement

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

July 21 close:

€11.906.

July 22 earnings-session close:

**€12.078, +1.44%.**

Intraday high:

€12.210.

Reuters recorded the shares **+1% early in the session**.

The scout's +2.8% figure is therefore excluded.

The actual move still qualifies as a clean positive earnings reaction.

The company delivered:

underlying profit slightly above expectations;

NII above expectations;

CET1 above expectations;

strong fees;

another buyback authorization.

There was no need for a positioning-based explanation.

The market largely traded the operating data.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder / diversified bank / capital-return story.**

Compared with UniCredit, Santander carries less single-transaction strategic overhang but more geographic/credit complexity.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. NII trajectory / Why it matters: Continued mid/high-single-digit growth would confirm that volumes and balance-sheet management can offset the ECB rate path.

2. Cost of risk / Why it matters: Persistent >115bp would indicate the LatAm credit cycle is becoming a larger earnings offset.

3. TSB integration / Why it matters: At least £400M of expected synergies must convert without repeated restructuring overruns.

4. Webster closing / Why it matters: Adds U.S. scale but increases execution and capital complexity.

5. Fee growth / Why it matters: Sustained growth above NII would materially improve earnings quality and reduce rate sensitivity.

BOTTOM LINE

Santander delivered a **clean improvement quarter**.

Underlying Q2 profit reached a record **€3.77B, +17%**.

NII rose **10%** and beat consensus.

Fees rose **12%**.

CET1 reached **14.0%**.

Efficiency improved further.

The market reacted positively.

The most important strategic signal is that Santander is producing strong earnings despite a European rate environment that should theoretically be becoming less supportive.

That tells us its current earnings strength is increasingly being driven by:

**volume + fees + efficiency + balance-sheet management**, not simply rates.

The main counterweight is credit.

Argentina and Brazil pushed cost of risk toward **115bp**, above the earlier target envelope.

The correct state is therefore:

**Improvement — European NII resilience and structural efficiency gains, partially offset by LatAm credit normalization and M&A execution risk.**

The initial rejection reflected a demanding setup, the €858M Ageas/AGI one-time gain flattering headline net income, higher-than-expected costs and continued Arval softness rather than weakness in core revenue or capital generation.
PM_READ: BNP delivered a strong diversified-bank print: Eurozone retail NII accelerated, Equities/Prime Services reached record levels, CET1 hit the 13% target early and capital reallocation is improving future earnings quality. The main debate has shifted from capital adequacy toward cost execution and how much of the stronger earnings can translate into higher distributions.
THEME_BUCKETS: Credit / Banks, Capital Markets, Rates Sensitivity, Margin / Cost Discipline

SOURCE_ROLE: Brief note

EXEC SUMMARY

BNP Paribas reported Q2 revenue of **€14.091B**, up 12.0% reported and 10.4% at constant scope and exchange rates. Net income attributable to shareholders reached **€4.345B**, up 33.4%, while operating income rose **15.9% to €5.057B**. Revenue and net income both exceeded company-compiled consensus.

The operating quality was stronger than the headline gain suggests. CIB revenue rose **12.7%**, led by **Equity & Prime Services +43%**, while Eurozone commercial banking benefited from double-digit NII growth. CET1 reached **13.0%**, up 20bp QoQ and ahead of the bank's prior timing expectations.

The principal accounting caveat is the **€858M capital gain from the AGI/Ageas transaction**. That contributed materially to the 33% net-income increase, but did not drive the core operating beat. Operating costs rose 10.4% to €7.986B, partly reflecting AXA IM integration, and remain the clearest execution watch.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 group revenue:

**€14.091B.**

YoY:

**+12.0% reported.**

Constant scope and exchange rates:

**+10.4%.**

The difference between the two growth rates reflects a combination of scope and FX.

It should **not** be described as a pure +160bp FX tailwind without a separate bridge.

CIB:

€5.281B.

Growth:

+12.7%.

CPBS:

€6.945B.

Growth:

+4.8%.

IPS:

+27.3% reported, but only +8.4% at constant scope and exchange rates because AXA IM materially changed the perimeter.

Consensus:

Net income:

€4.345B versus **€4.21B** consensus.

Revenue:

€14.091B versus **€13.545B** consensus.

Equity & Prime Services:

approximately €1.4B versus around **€1.1B** expected.

Those are genuine operating beats even after removing the Ageas gain.

EPS / Earnings:

Net income, group share:

**€4.345B**.

YoY:

+33.4%.

Pre-tax income:

**€6.063B**, +33%.

Operating income:

**€5.057B**, +15.9%.

Gross operating income:

**€6.105B**.

The operating-income growth is the cleaner measure of the quarter because reported net income contains the Ageas/AGI transaction gain.

Profitability / Margins:

Q2 operating costs:

**€7.986B**, +10.4%.

Q2 cost/income ratio:

**56.7%**, down approximately 80bp YoY.

The tape's ~60% figure is the **2026 full-year objective**, not the Q2 actual.

BNP remains committed to:

approximately 60% C/I in 2026;

below 56% in 2028.

The group has increased its structural efficiency ambition, with management discussing **€1B of additional savings**, around 80% of which has already been identified according to the supplied call tape.

Key KPI:

The most useful forward banking indicator is not aggregate group NII.

It is **Eurozone commercial-bank NII normalization**.

Management described double-digit NII growth in the Eurozone banks, with strong contributions from France and Belgium, while Italy remained weaker.

That matters because BNP entered the easing cycle with a different liability/hedge profile from many peers.

The current improvement comes from:

yield-curve normalization;
deposit repricing;
commercial margins;
balance-sheet activity.

The better framework is therefore:

**ECB path × deposit beta × hedge roll-off × loan volumes × local competition.**

Guidance headline:

BNP reaffirmed:

2026 RoTE:

**12%.**

2028 RoTE:

**>13%.**

2026 cost/income ratio:

approximately **60%.**

2028 cost/income:

**<56%.**

2025-28 EPS CAGR:

**>10%.**

The next strategic plan will be presented in February 2027.

Capital allocation:

CET1 reached **13.0%**, up 20bp QoQ.

Management explicitly treats 13% as the target level rather than a floor that needs to be built materially higher.

That changes the capital-return debate.

With the ratio already at target, incremental earnings and asset disposals create more room for:

dividends;
buybacks;
or selective acquisitions.

The interim dividend was raised to **€3.23/share**.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed.

Adjustment issue:

The major item is the **€858M AGI/Ageas transaction capital gain**.

Net exceptional items were approximately **€761M** in Q2.

This is a genuine one-time transaction gain.

It is not recurring trading revenue and should not be treated as an equity mark.

Removing it materially reduces the apparent magnitude of the net-income beat.

However, the core operating print remains strong because:

revenue beat consensus;

CIB revenue accelerated;

equities beat substantially;

commercial-bank NII improved;

operating income rose 15.9%;

CET1 strengthened.

PM interpretation:

The quarter is a case where:

**reported earnings quality = mixed**

but

**underlying operating quality = strong.**

The market was right to discount the full +33% headline profit growth.

It would be wrong to infer from that accounting adjustment that the operating quarter itself was weak.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The main strategic development is that BNP has now achieved the **capital constraint before achieving the final cost objective**.

That changes the order of the equity debate.

Previously:

**build CET1 → then distribute.**

Now:

**CET1 already at 13% → cost execution / earnings growth determine incremental distribution capacity.**

The capital reallocation programme also supports this.

The Ageas/AGI transaction generated a capital gain and future recurring income.

The Athlon acquisition is expected to contribute roughly **€200M of annual recurring earnings after integration**, versus around €30M from BMCI before disposal according to management's call comments in the supplied tape.

Revision direction: Upward revisions.

Operating revenue and capital assumptions improve.

Costs remain the main negative revision risk.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

BNP is the most diversified European-bank print in this cohort.

UBS:

wealth + capital markets.

HSBC:

Asia + banking NII + transaction banking.

Santander:

NII + fees + geographic diversification.

BNP:

**Eurozone NII + CIB + wealth/asset management + capital reallocation.**

That diversification matters because Q2 did not rely solely on rates.

Equity and Prime Services revenue increased **43%**.

CIB revenue rose 13%.

IPS grew organically even after adjusting for AXA IM.

Retail NII simultaneously improved.

That is a higher-quality earnings mix than a one-factor Eurozone rate trade.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The strongest positive signal is not the 33% net-income increase.

It is the breadth of the operating beat.

The second is **CET1 at 13%**.

The third is **Equity and Prime Services +43%**.

The weakest signal is cost.

Reuters specifically highlighted expenses as the soft point, with AXA IM integration and Arval weakness reducing the quality of the headline surprise.

That explains more of the share-price response than the scout's original “simple sold-the-news” framing.

The market appears to have asked:

**How much of the revenue acceleration becomes sustainable EPS once the Ageas gain disappears and integration costs normalize?**

State transition: Improvement

**€106.78.**

The likely interpretation is a combination of:

one-time gain flattering reported earnings;

costs above expectations;

Arval weakness;

strong pre-print positioning.

But the next-day rebound and subsequent analyst upgrades indicate that the market did not reject the core operating thesis.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder / diversified bank / capital-return story.**

The rerating case increasingly depends on converting capital surplus and strong revenue growth into lower costs and higher shareholder distributions.

RATES / CAPITAL-MARKETS CHECK

Eurozone commercial banks:

double-digit NII growth.

Italy:

weaker.

+12.7%.

+43%.

approximately flat against a high base.

BNP benefits from both:

**rate normalization**

and

**market volatility/activity.**

That gives it a broader earnings hedge than pure retail banks.

CREDIT / CAPITAL CHECK

Cost of risk:

**39bp.**

FY guide:

below 40bp.

CET1:

**13.0%.**

Conclusion:

credit remains benign enough not to constrain capital returns.

The principal constraint is now cost execution rather than solvency or credit deterioration.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. Eurozone NII / Why it matters: Sustained double-digit growth would confirm that BNP's hedge/deposit structure remains favorable through the ECB easing cycle.

2. Cost/income ratio / Why it matters: Revenue acceleration must translate into the sub-56% 2028 target rather than being absorbed by integration costs.

3. AXA IM integration / Why it matters: The acquisition materially increases IPS scale but also raises near-term expenses.

4. Equity & Prime Services / Why it matters: Q2 +43% is exceptional and should not be extrapolated without evidence of sustained client activity.

5. Capital distribution / Why it matters: CET1 is already at target, so excess capital should increasingly show up in dividends or buybacks.

BOTTOM LINE

BNP delivered a **strong underlying improvement quarter with mixed headline earnings quality**.

Revenue rose **12%**.

CIB rose **12.7%**.

Equity & Prime Services rose **43%**.

Operating income rose **15.9%**.

CET1 reached **13.0%** ahead of plan.

Cost of risk remained contained at **39bp**.

The €858M Ageas/AGI gain materially inflated the reported 33% net-income growth, and operating costs remained the main weak point.

That explains why the stock initially fell **2.83%** despite the beat.

The right state is therefore:

**Improvement — diversified revenue acceleration and capital normalization, with cost conversion now the key remaining proof point.**

The investment question has moved from:

**Can BNP build enough capital?**

to:

**Can BNP convert its broader revenue growth into lower costs and accelerated distributions now that the 13% CET1 target has already been achieved?**

EXEC SUMMARY

UniCredit reported Q2 net profit of **€2.9B**, or approximately **€3.1B excluding the €245M Commerzbank-related trading and hedging charge**. The bank's broader adjusted framework showed net profit growth of roughly 22% YoY, while reported profit was weaker against a prior-year quarter containing a substantial positive below-the-line one-off.

Q2 revenue reached **€6.5B, +7% YoY**, or approximately €6.7B and +11% excluding the Commerzbank trading item. Core revenue rose 6%, fees plus net insurance income rose 14%, loans and deposits both grew 8%, and NII accelerated **2% sequentially**.

Management raised FY26 net-profit ambition to **well above €11B**, or approximately **€11.5B excluding integration costs**, while FY28 and FY30 profit ambitions moved to above €13B and €15B respectively. The negative stock reaction was therefore not an operating rejection: it was a repricing of the **Commerzbank capital-allocation decision**.

OFFICIAL EARNINGS DETAIL

Revenue / Sales:

Q2 reported revenue:

**€6.5B.**

YoY:

**+7%.**

Excluding the **-€245M Commerzbank-related trading one-off**, primarily hedging and funding costs connected with the increased position:

approximately **€6.7B**.

YoY growth on that basis:

**+11%.**

Core revenue:

**+6% YoY.**

UniCredit separately reports broader adjusted metrics that normalize additional items, including Russia, loan-loss-provision phasing and the prior-year below-the-line gain.

Those measures should remain distinct from the simpler ex-Commerzbank trading bridge.

EPS / Earnings:

Reported Q2 net profit:

**€2.9B.**

Ex Commerzbank trading item:

approximately **€3.1B**.

H1 net profit:

approximately **€6.3B** on the corresponding ex-trading basis.

The reported YoY profit decline is largely an optical comparison against a prior-year quarter containing approximately **€650M of positive below-the-line one-offs**.

On UniCredit's adjusted basis, underlying profit growth remained above 20%.

Profitability / Margins:

Q2 RoTE:

approximately **23%-24%**.

H1 RoTE:

approximately **24%**.

H1 cost/income:

**34%.**

Costs:

down approximately **1% YoY**, despite continued investment in technology, AI and client-facing resources.

This remains one of the strongest cost/productivity profiles in European banking.

Key KPI:

The cleanest earnings KPI is now:

**NII trajectory + fee growth + capital generation.**

NII increased **2% sequentially**.

Management expects another sequential improvement in H2 as margins stabilize and rate dynamics become more supportive.

The importance of that signal is enhanced by:

loans +8%;

deposits +8%;

fees/insurance +14%.

UniCredit is therefore not relying simply on deposit spread.

Guidance headline:

FY26:

net profit **well above €11B**.

Approximately **€11.5B excluding integration costs**.

FY28:

**well above €13B**.

FY30:

**well above €15B**.

Importantly, the FY28 and FY30 ambitions disclosed with Q2 are **before full consolidation of Commerzbank**.

That means management is presenting Commerzbank as incremental to an already-rising standalone earnings base.

Capital allocation:

CET1 ended Q2 at **14.3%**.

Excluding the temporary RWA impact linked to the strategic portfolio:

**14.5%.**

Including anticipated Danish Compromise treatment and reversal of the temporary impact:

approximately **15.0%**.

UniCredit accrued approximately **€2.3B of shareholder distributions in Q2** and €4.7B in H1.

The key issue is no longer whether the standalone bank generates sufficient capital.

It clearly does.

The question is **where that capital goes**.

ACCOUNTING / EPS QUALITY CHECK

Reported earnings quality:

Mixed but operationally strong.

Adjustment issue:

There are two major comparison items.

**1. Q2 2026 Commerzbank trading item**

Approximately:

**-€245M.**

Nature:

hedging and funding costs connected to the increased Commerzbank stake and associated protection.

Reported inside trading income.

This is directly related to strategic capital deployment rather than normal customer operations.

**2. Q2 2025 positive one-off**

Approximately:

**+€650M below the line.**

This materially flatters the prior-year reported earnings comparison and explains why statutory Q2 profit can decline while underlying profit grows more than 20%.

PM interpretation:

This is an **adversarial accounting bridge in reverse**.

Unlike BNP, where a current-quarter gain inflated headline profit, UniCredit's current-quarter headline understates the underlying earnings momentum because:

the current quarter contains acquisition-related hedging costs;

the comparator contains a favorable prior-year one-off.

The better operating lens is therefore:

core revenue;
NII;
fees/insurance;
costs;
cost of risk;
adjusted profit.

The approximately €140M Banca Progetto / Romania item is small relative to group earnings and does not alter the interpretation.

GUIDANCE / CAPITAL ALLOCATION

The Commerzbank transaction changes UniCredit's equity story more than the Q2 operating results do.

At the end of the tender process UniCredit controlled economic interests equivalent to roughly **47.6% of Commerzbank's share capital**, close to half of the voting rights after treasury-share adjustment.

The strategic logic is clear:

combine UniCredit/HypoVereinsbank and Commerzbank;
increase German scale;
centralize product factories;
generate procurement and technology savings;
cross-sell trade finance, markets and other products.

Management's expected pre-tax value creation has increased to approximately **€1.2B**.

Crucially, the supplied tape indicates roughly **40%-50% of the synergies are non-headcount related**, including procurement, technology and platform consolidation.

That gives the integration thesis better quality than a pure branch/headcount cost-cutting exercise.

The price is capital.

Full consolidation is expected to consume approximately **200bp of CET1**.

That makes the equation:

**standalone organic capital generation**
→ **Commerzbank capital deployment**
→ **integration investment**
→ **synergy capture**
→ **future distributions.**

Revision direction: **Mix-shift revisions.**

Standalone earnings estimates move upward.

Near-term capital-return assumptions become less straightforward.

Longer-term EPS estimates depend increasingly on Commerzbank integration economics.

PROFESSIONAL MARKET COMMENTARY

UniCredit is arguably the strongest **standalone operating bank** in the European cohort.

The combination is difficult to match:

RoTE around 24%;

34% cost/income;

17bp cost of risk;

8% loan growth;

8% deposit growth;

double-digit fee/insurance growth.

That is why the stock's almost 5% decline cannot reasonably be interpreted as the market rejecting Q2 earnings.

The market rejected—or at least demanded a higher risk premium for—the **capital-allocation choice**.

This is fundamentally different from Santander.

Santander's M&A adds TSB/Webster incrementally to a diversified existing bank.

UniCredit is attempting to deploy a much larger proportion of its excess capital into a transformative German control transaction.

The standalone business may be becoming safer while the equity simultaneously becomes strategically more complicated.

COMMENT — ANALYST LAYER

The most important positive signal is **NII +2% sequentially**.

This is the correct Eurozone rate-cycle read-through.

The second is fees and insurance +14%.

The third is cost discipline.

The strongest negative is the Commerzbank capital bridge.

A roughly 200bp CET1 hit is large enough to change the immediate shareholder-distribution profile even if the transaction is ultimately accretive.

The second negative is negotiation risk.

Commerzbank's own strong operating results improve its bargaining position and make the transaction less obviously one-sided.

State transition: Continuation

HF TAKE — STRATEGIST LAYER

Print reaction function:

July 22 Milan close:

approximately **€83.21**.

July 23 close:

**€79.22, -4.8%.**

The stock traded as low as approximately €78.86 during the session.

It recovered about 1.5% the following day.

The proper reaction classification is therefore:

**release-specific negative / strategic-overhang driven.**

This was not a normal elevated-bar earnings fade.

The market received genuinely new information about:

Commerzbank control intention;

roughly 200bp CET1 consumption;

buyback flexibility;

integration cost;

execution duration.

Those are fundamental equity variables.

The distinction is:

**operating fundamentals positive**
versus
**capital-allocation information negative.**

That tension can persist rather than automatically converging within days.

Stock identity:

**Quality compounder / high-return bank / capital-allocation setup-risk stock.**

Without Commerzbank, the investment case would be unusually clean.

With Commerzbank, valuation depends on whether Orcel can replicate the UniCredit transformation playbook at a much larger German institution.

RATES / NII CHECK

Q2 NII:

+2% sequentially.

Client spread:

approximately 138bp H1.

Deposit pass-through:

approximately 30%.

Loan growth:

+8%.

Deposit growth:

+8%.

Management:

expects sequential H2 NII acceleration.

This is among the strongest pieces of evidence in the European-bank batch against a simplistic:

**ECB easing → bank NII falls.**

The correct framework remains:

**policy rate × deposit beta × hedge/ALCO book × loan growth × mix.**

UniCredit currently has enough volume growth and balance-sheet discipline to offset the policy-rate headwind.

CAPITAL / COMMERZBANK CHECK

Standalone CET1:

14.3%.

Ex temporary impact:

14.5%.

Pro forma Danish Compromise / temporary reversal:

~15%.

Full Commerzbank consolidation:

approximately **-200bp**.

Strategic objective:

approximately **15% RoAC** on deployed capital.

Expected synergies:

approximately **€1.2B**.

The transaction therefore needs to generate sufficiently high earnings to compensate investors for:

lower immediate excess capital;

integration risk;

German political resistance;

potentially weaker buyback optionality.

This is now the central stock-specific arbitration.

KEY ISSUES TO MONITOR

1. H2 NII acceleration / Why it matters: Confirms the standalone earnings thesis remains intact through the ECB cycle.

2. Commerzbank regulatory approval / Why it matters: Determines whether the strategic capital bridge actually enters group accounts.

3. CET1 after consolidation / Why it matters: A fall materially below ~13% would weaken management's distribution thesis.

4. €1.2B synergy delivery / Why it matters: The transaction's valuation depends on rapid realization of product, technology and procurement benefits.

5. Buyback/distribution policy / Why it matters: The market needs evidence that Commerzbank does not permanently absorb UniCredit's exceptional standalone capital generation.

BOTTOM LINE

UniCredit delivered another **exceptionally strong operating quarter**, but the state transition remains **Continuation** because the business was already performing at record levels.

Q2 revenue rose **7%**, or **11% excluding Commerzbank-related trading costs**.

Underlying net profit was approximately **€3.1B**.

NII accelerated **2% sequentially**.

Fees and insurance rose **14%**.

RoTE remained around **23%-24%**.

Cost/income was only **34%**.

Cost of risk remained just **17bp**.

Management raised the standalone earnings outlook again.

Yet the stock fell **4.8%**.

That is not an earnings contradiction.

The market is separating two questions:

**Is UniCredit's standalone bank getting better?**

Yes.

**Is taking control of Commerzbank the best use of its extraordinary capital generation?**

Still unresolved.

The investment case has therefore moved from **operating execution risk to capital-allocation execution risk**.
